| THE [[history\|HISTORY]] OF [[King\|KING]] | LEAR |
| THE QUARTO TEXT | [[King Lear]] first appeared in print in a quarto of 1608. A substantially different text appeared in the 1623 Folio. Until the first appearance of the [[Oxford]] text, editors, assuming that each of these early texts imperfectly represented a single play, conflated them. But research conducted mainly during the 1970s and 1980s confirms an earlier view that the 1608 quarto represents the play as Shakespeare originally wrote it, and the 1623 Folio as he substantially revised it. He revised other plays, too, but usually by making many small changes in the dialogue and adding or omitting passages, as in [[Hamlet]], [[Troilus]] and [[Cressida]], and [[Othello]]. For these plays we print the revised text in so far as it can be ascertained. But in [[King Lear]] revisions are not simply local but structural, too; conflation, as Harley Granville-Barker wrote, ‘may make for redundancy or confusion’, so we print an edited version of each text. The first, printed in the following pages, represents the play as Shakespeare first conceived it, probably before it was performed.<br>The story of a [[King\|king]] who, angry with the failure of his virtuous youngest daughter ([[Cordelia]]) to respond as he desires in a [[Love\|love]]-test, divides his kingdom between her two malevolent sisters (Gonoril and [[Regan]]), had been often told; Shakespeare would have come upon it in Holinshed’s Chronicles and in A [[Mirror]] for Magistrates while reading for his plays on English [[history]]. It is told also (though briefly) in [[Edmund]] Spenser’s Faerie Queene (Book 2, canto 10), and had been dramatized in a play of unknown authorship—The True Chronicle [[history\|History]] of [[King]] Leir and his three daughters—published in 1605, but probably written some fifteen years earlier. This play particularly gave Shakespeare much, including suggestions for the characters of Lear’s loyal servant, [[Kent]], and of Gonoril’s husband, Albany, and her steward, Oswald; for the [[Storm\|storm]]; for Lear’s kneeling to [[Cordelia]]; and for many details of language. Nevertheless, his play is a highly original creation. Lear’s [[Madness\|madness]] and the harrowing series of disasters in [[King Lear]]’s final stages are of Shakespeare’s invention, and he complicates the plot by adding the story (based on an episode of Sir Philip Sidney’s Arcadia) of [[Gloucester]] and his two sons, [[Edmund]] and [[Edgar]]. [[Edgar]]’s [[Love\|love]] and loyalty to the father who, failing to see the [[Truth\|truth]], has rejected him in favour of the villainous [[Edmund]] makes him a counterpart to [[Cordelia]]; and the horrific blinding of [[Gloucester]] brought about by [[Edmund]] creates a physical parallel to Lear’s [[Madness\|madness]] which reaches its consummation in the scene (Sc. 20) at Dover Cliff when the mad and the blind old men commune together.<br>The clear-eyed intensity of Shakespeare’s tragic vision in [[King Lear]] has been too much for some audiences, and Nahum Tate’s adaptation, which gave the play a happy ending, held the stage from 1681 to 1843; since then, increased understanding of Shakespeare’s stagecraft along with a greater seriousness in theatre audiences has assisted in the rehabilitation of a play that is now recognized as one of the profoundest of all artistic explorations of the human condition.<br><br>In the text which follows, the Quarto scene numbers are followed by the equivalent Folio act and scene numbers in parentheses. There is no equivalent to Sc. 17 in the Folio. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>LEAR, [[King]] of Britain<br>GONORIL, Lear’s eldest daughter<br>Duke of ALBANY, her husband<br>[[Regan\|REGAN]], Lear’s second daughter<br>Duke of CORNWALL, her husband<br>[[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]], Lear’s youngest daughter<br>Earl of [[Kent\|KENT]], later disguised as Caius<br>Earl of [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]<br>[[Edgar\|EDGAR]], elder son of [[Gloucester]], later disguised as Tom o’ Bedlam<br>[[Edmund\|EDMUND]], bastard son of [[Gloucester]] |
| OLD | MAN, a tenant of [[Gloucester]]<br>CURAN, [[Gloucester]]’s retainer<br>Lear’s [[Fool\|FOOL]]<br>OSWALD, Gonoril’s steward<br>Three SERVANTS of Cornwall<br>DOCTOR, attendant on [[Cordelia]]<br>Three CAPTAINS<br>A HERALD<br>A KNIGHT<br>A MESSENGER<br>Gentlemen, servants, soldiers, followers, trumpeters, others<br><br>The [[history\|History]] of [[King Lear]]<br><br>Sc. 1 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and [[Edmund]] the bastard |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I thought the [[King]] had more affected the Duke of Albany than Cornwall. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | It did always seem so to us, but now in the division of the kingdoms it appears not which of the Dukes he values most; for equalities are so weighed that curiosity in neither can make choice of either’s moiety. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Is not this your son, my lord? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | His breeding, sir, hath been at my charge. I have so often blushed to acknowledge him that now I am brazed to it. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I cannot conceive you. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sir, this young fellow’s mother could, whereupon she grew round-wombed and had indeed, sir, a son for her cradle ere she had a husband for her bed. Do you smell a fault? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I cannot wish the fault undone, the issue of it being so proper. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But I have, sir, a son by order of law, some year elder than this, who yet is no dearer in my account. Though this knave came something saucily into the world before he was sent for, yet was his mother fair, there was good sport at his making, and the whoreson must be acknowledged. (To [[Edmund]]) Do you know this noble gentleman, [[Edmund]]? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | No, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[Edmund]]) My lord of [[Kent]]. Remember him hereafter as my honourable friend. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | (to [[Kent]]) My services to your lordship. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I must [[Love\|love]] you, and sue to know you better. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Sir, I shall study deserving. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[Kent]]) He hath been out nine years, and away he shall again. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sound a sennet |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is coming. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter one bearing a coronet, then [[King Lear]], then the Dukes of Albany and Cornwall; next Gonoril, [[Regan]], [[Cordelia]], with followers |
| LEAR | Attend my lords of France and Burgundy, [[Gloucester]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I shall, my liege. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | Meantime we will express our darker purposes.<br>The map there. Know we have divided<br>In three our kingdom, and ’tis our first intent<br>To shake all cares and business off our state,<br>Confirming them on younger years.<br>The two great princes, France and Burgundy—<br>Great rivals in our youngest daughter’s [[Love\|love]]—<br>Long in our [[Court\|court]] have made their amorous sojourn,<br>And here are to be answered. Tell me, my daughters,<br>Which of you shall we say doth [[Love\|love]] us most,<br>That we our largest bounty may extend<br>Where merit doth most challenge it?<br>Gonoril, our eldest born, speak first. |
| GONORIL | Sir, I do [[Love\|love]] you more than words can wield the<br>matter;<br>Dearer than eyesight, space, or liberty;<br>Beyond what can be valued, rich or rare;<br>No less than life; with grace, health, [[Beauty\|beauty]], [[Honour\|honour]];<br>As much as [[Child\|child]] e’er loved, or father, friend;<br>A [[Love\|love]] that makes breath poor and speech unable.<br>Beyond all manner of so much I [[Love\|love]] you. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (aside)<br>What shall [[Cordelia]] do? [[Love]] and be silent. |
| LEAR | (to Gonoril)<br>Of all these bounds even from this line to this,<br>With shady forests and wide skirted meads,<br>We make thee lady. To thine and Albany’s issue<br>Be this perpetual.—What says our second daughter?<br>Our dearest [[Regan]], wife to Cornwall, speak. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sir, I am made<br>Of the [[Self\|self]]-same mettle that my sister is,<br>And prize me at her worth. In my true heart<br>I find she names my very deed of [[Love\|love]]—<br>Only she came short, that I profess<br>Myself an enemy to all other joys<br>Which the most precious square of sense possesses,<br>And find I am [[Alone\|alone]] felicitate<br>In your dear highness’ [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (aside) Then poor [[Cordelia]]—<br>And yet not so, since I am sure my [[Love\|love]]’s<br>More richer than my tongue. |
| LEAR | (to [[Regan]])<br>To thee and thine hereditary ever<br>Remain this ample third of our fair kingdom, No less in space, validity, and pleasure<br>Than that confirmed on Gonoril. (To [[Cordelia]]) But<br>now our joy,<br>Although the last, not least in our dear [[Love\|love]]:<br>What can you say to win a third more opulent<br>Than your sisters? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Nothing, my lord. |
| LEAR | How? Nothing can come of nothing. Speak again. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Unhappy that I am, I cannot heave<br>My heart into my mouth. I [[Love\|love]] your majesty<br>According to my bond, nor more nor less. |
| LEAR | Go to, go to, mend your speech a little<br>Lest it may mar your fortunes. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Good my lord,<br>You have begot me, bred me, loved me.<br>I return those duties back as are right fit—<br>Obey you, [[Love\|love]] you, and most [[Honour\|honour]] you.<br>Why have my sisters husbands if they say<br>They [[Love\|love]] you all? Haply when I shall wed<br>That lord whose hand must take my plight shall carry<br>Half my [[Love\|love]] with him, half my care and duty.<br>Sure, I shall never marry like my sisters,<br>To [[Love\|love]] my father all. |
| LEAR | But goes this with thy heart? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Ay, good my lord. |
| LEAR | So young and so untender? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | So young, my lord, and true. |
| LEAR | [[Well]], let it be so. Thy [[Truth\|truth]] then he thy dower;<br>For by the sacred radiance of the sun,<br>The mysteries of Hecate and the [[Night\|night]],<br>By all the operation of the orbs<br>From whom we do exist and cease to be,<br>Here I disclaim all my paternal care,<br>Propinquity, and property of [[Blood\|blood]],<br>And as a stranger to my heart and me<br>Hold thee from this for ever. The barbarous Scythian,<br>Or he that makes his generation<br>Messes to gorge his appetite,<br>Shall be as [[Well\|well]] neighboured, pitied, and relieved<br>As thou, my sometime daughter. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my +liege- |
| LEAR | Peace, [[Kent]]. Come not between the dragon and his<br>wrath.<br>I loved her most, and thought to set my rest |
| *(didascalia)* | On her kind nursery. To [[Cordelia]] Hence, and avoid |
| LEAR | my sight!—<br>So be my [[Grave\|grave]] my peace as here I give<br>Her father’s heart from her. Call France. Who stirs?<br>Call Burgundy. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| LEAR | Cornwall and Albany,<br>With my two daughters’ dowers digest this third.<br>Let [[Pride\|pride]], which she calls plainness, marry her.<br>I do invest you jointly in my power,<br>Pre-eminence, and all the large effects<br>That troop with majesty. Ourself by monthly course,<br>With reservation of an hundred knights<br>By you to be sustained, shall our abode<br>Make with you by due turns. Only we still retain<br>The name and all the additions to a [[King\|king]].<br>The sway, revenue, execution of the rest,<br>Beloved sons, be yours; which to confirm,<br>This crownet part betwixt you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Royal Lear,<br>Whom I have ever honoured as my [[King\|king]],<br>Loved as my father, as my master followed,<br>As my great patron thought on in my prayers— |
| LEAR | The bow is bent and drawn; make from the shaft. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Let it fall rather, though the fork invade<br>The region of my heart. Be [[Kent]] unmannerly<br>When Lear is mad. What wilt thou do, old man?<br>Think’st thou that duty shall have dread to speak<br>When power to flattery bows? To plainness [[Honour\|honour]]’s<br>bound<br>When majesty stoops to folly. Reverse thy doom,<br>And in thy best consideration check<br>This hideous rashness. Answer my life my judgement,<br>Thy youngest daughter does not [[Love\|love]] thee least,<br>Nor are those empty-hearted whose low sound<br>Reverbs no hollowness. |
| LEAR | [[Kent]], on thy life, no more! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My life I never held but as a pawn<br>To wage against thy enemies, nor [[Fear\|fear]] to lose it,<br>Thy safety being the motive. |
| LEAR | Out of my sight! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | See better, Lear, and let me still remain<br>The true blank of thine eye. |
| LEAR | Now, by [[Apollo]]- |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Now, by [[Apollo]], [[King]], thou swear’st thy gods in vain. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR making to strike himl |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Vassal, recreant! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Do, kill thy physician,<br>And the fee bestow upon the foul disease.<br>Revoke thy doom, or whilst I can vent clamour<br>From my throat I’ll tell thee thou dost evil. |
| LEAR | Hear me; on thy allegiance hear me!<br>Since thou hast sought to make us break our vow,<br>Which we durst never yet, and with strayed [[Pride\|pride]]<br>To come between our sentence and our power,<br>Which nor our [[Nature\|nature]] nor our place can bear,<br>Our potency made good take thy reward:<br>Four days we do allot thee for provision<br>To shield thee from dis-eases of the world,<br>And on the fifth to turn thy hated back<br>Upon our kingdom. If on the next day following<br>Thy banished trunk be found in our dominions,<br>The moment is thy [[Death\|death]]. Away! By Jupiter,<br>This shall not be revoked. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, fare thee [[Well\|well]], [[King]]; since thus thou wilt<br>appear,<br>[[Friendship]] lives hence, and banishment is here.<br>(To [[Cordelia]]) The gods to their protection take thee,<br>maid,<br>That rightly thinks, and hast most justly said.<br>(To Gonoril and [[Regan]])<br>And your large speeches may your deeds approve,<br>That good effects may [[Spring\|spring]] from words of [[Love\|love]].<br>Thus [[Kent]], O princes, bids you all adieu;<br>He’ll shape his old course in a country new. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[King of France]] and the Duke of |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Burgundy, with the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Here’s France and Burgundy, my noble lord. |
| LEAR | My lord of Burgundy,<br>We first address towards you, who with a [[King\|king]]<br>Hath rivalled for our daughter: what in the least<br>Will you require in present dower with her<br>Or cease your [[Quest\|quest]] of [[Love\|love]]? |
| BURGUNDY | Royal majesty,<br>I crave no more than what your highness offered;<br>Nor will you tender less. |
| LEAR | Right noble Burgundy,<br>When she was dear to us we did hold her so;<br>But now her price is fallen. Sir, there she stands.<br>If aught within that little seeming substance,<br>Or all of it, with our displeasure pieced,<br>And nothing else, may fitly like your grace,<br>She’s there, and she is yours. |
| BURGUNDY | I know no answer. |
| LEAR | Sir, will you with those infirmities she owes,<br>Unfriended, new-adopted to our hate,<br>Covered with our curse and strangered with our oath,<br>Take her or leave her? |
| BURGUNDY | Pardon me, royal sir.<br>Election makes not up on such conditions. |
| LEAR | Then leave her, sir; for by the power that made me,<br>I tell you all her wealth. (To France) For you, great<br>[[King]],<br>I would not from your [[Love\|love]] make such a stray<br>To match you where I hate, therefore beseech you<br>To avert your liking a more worthier way<br>Than on a wretch whom [[Nature\|nature]] is ashamed<br>Almost to acknowledge hers. |
| FRANCE | This is most strange, that she that even but now<br>Was your best object, the argument of your praise,<br>Balm of your age, most best, most dearest,<br>Should in this trice of [[Time\|time]] commit a thing<br>So monstrous to dismantle<br>So many folds of favour. Sure, her offence<br>Must be of such unnatural degree<br>That monsters it, or your fore-vouched affections<br>Fall’n into taint; which to believe of her<br>Must be a [[Faith\|faith]] that reason without miracle<br>Could never plant in me. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear)<br>I yet beseech your majestyIf for I want that glib and oily art<br>To speak and purpose not—since what I [[Well\|well]] intend,<br>I’ll do’t before I speak—that you acknow<br>It is no vicious blot, murder, or foulness,<br>No unclean action or dishonoured step<br>That hath deprived me of your grace and favour,<br>But even the want of that for which I am rich—<br>A still-soliciting eye, and such a tongue<br>As I am glad I have not, though not to have it<br>Hath lost me in your liking. |
| LEAR | Go to, go to.<br>Better thou hadst not been born than not to have<br>pleased me better. |
| FRANCE | Is it no more but this—a tardiness in [[Nature\|nature]],<br>That often [[Leaves\|leaves]] the [[history]] unspoke<br>That it intends to do?—My lord of Burgundy,<br>What say you to the lady? [[Love]] is not [[Love\|love]]<br>When it is mingled with respects that stands<br>Aloof from the entire point. Will you have her?<br>She is herself a dower. |
| BURGUNDY | Royal Lear,<br>Give but that portion which yourself proposed,<br>And here I take [[Cordelia]] by the hand,<br>Duchess of Burgundy— |
| LEAR | Nothing. I have sworn. |
| BURGUNDY | (to [[Cordelia]])<br>I am sorry, then, you have so lost a father<br>That you must lose a husband. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Peace be with Burgundy; since that respects<br>Of fortune are his [[Love\|love]], I shall not be his wife. |
| FRANCE | Fairest [[Cordelia]], that art most rich, being poor;<br>Most choice, forsaken; and most loved, despised:<br>Thee and thy virtues here I seize upon.<br>Be it lawful, I take up what’s cast away.<br>Gods, gods! ‘Tis strange that from their cold’st neglect<br>My [[Love\|love]] should kindle to inflamed respect.—<br>Thy dowerless daughter, [[King]], thrown to my chance,<br>Is [[Queen\|queen]] of us, of ours, and our fair France.<br>Not all the dukes in wat’rish Burgundy<br>Shall buy this unprized precious maid of me.—<br>Bid them farewell, [[Cordelia]], though unkind.<br>Thou losest here, a better where to find. |
| LEAR | Thou hast her, France. Let her be thine, for we<br>Have no such daughter, nor shall ever see<br>That face of hers again. Therefore be gone,<br>Without our grace, our [[Love\|love]], our benison.—<br>Come, noble Burgundy.<br>[Flourish.! Exeunt Lear and Burgundy, then |
| *(didascalia)* | Albany, Cornwall, [[Gloucester]], [[Edmund]], |
| LEAR | and followers |
| FRANCE | (to [[Cordelia]]) Bid farewell to your sisters. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Ye jewels of our father, with washed eyes<br>[[Cordelia]] [[Leaves\|leaves]] you. I know you what you are,<br>And like a sister am most loath to call<br>Your faults as they are named. Use [[Well\|well]] our father.<br>To your professed bosoms I commit him.<br>But yet, alas, stood I within his grace<br>I would prefer him to a better place.<br>So farewell to you both. |
| GONORIL | Prescribe not us our duties. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Let your study<br>Be to content your lord, who hath received you<br>At fortune’s alms. You have obedience scanted,<br>And [[Well\|well]] are worth the worst that you have wanted. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | [[Time]] shall unfold what pleated cunning hides.<br>Who covers faults, at last shame them derides.<br>[[Well]] may you prosper. |
| FRANCE | Come, fair [[Cordelia]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt France and [[Cordelia]] |
| GONORIL | Sister, it is not a little I have to say of what most nearly appertains to us both. I think our father will hence tonight. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | That’s most certain, and with you. Next month with us. |
| GONORIL | You see how full of changes his age is. The observation we have made of it hath not been little. He always loved our sister most, and with what poor judgement he hath now cast her off appears too gross. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | ’Tis the infirmity of his age; yet he hath ever but slenderly known himself. |
| GONORIL | The best and soundest of his [[Time\|time]] hath been but rash; then must we look to receive from his age not [[Alone\|alone]] the imperfection of long-engrafted condition, but therewithal unruly waywardness that infirm and choleric years bring with them. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Such unconstant starts are we like to have from him as this of [[Kent]]’s banishment. |
| GONORIL | There is further compliment of leave-taking between France and him. Pray, let’s hit together. If our father carry authority with such dispositions as he bears, this last surrender of his will but offend us. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | We shall further think on’t. |
| GONORIL | We must do something, and l’th’ heat. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 2 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edmund]] the bastard |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Thou, [[Nature\|nature]], art my goddess. To thy law<br>My services are bound. Wherefore should I<br>Stand in the plague of custom and permit<br>The curiosity of nations to deprive me<br>For that I am some twelve or fourteen moonshines<br>Lag of a brother? Why ‘bastard’? Wherefore ‘base’,<br>When my dimensions are as [[Well\|well]] compact,<br>My mind as generous, and my shape as true<br>As honest madam’s issue?<br>Why brand they us with ‘base, base bastardy’,<br>Who in the lusty stealth of [[Nature\|nature]] take<br>More composition and fierce quality<br>Than doth within a stale, dull-eyed bed go<br>To the creating a whole tribe of fops<br>Got ’tween a [[Sleep\|sleep]] and wake? [[Well]] then,<br>Legitimate [[Edgar]], I must have your land.<br>Our father’s [[Love\|love]] is to the bastard [[Edmund]]<br>As to the legitimate. [[Well]], my legitimate, if<br>This letter [[Speed\|speed]] and my invention thrive,<br>[[Edmund]] the base shall to th’ legitimate.<br>I grow, I prosper. Now gods, stand up for bastards! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]]. [[Edmund]] reads a letter |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Kent]] banished thus, and France in choler parted,<br>And the [[King]] gone tonight, subscribed his power,<br>Confined to exhibition—all this done<br>Upon the gad?—[[Edmund]], how now? What news? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | So please your lordship, none. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Why so earnestly seek you to put up that letter? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I know no news, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What paper were you reading? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Nothing., my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No? What needs then that terrible dispatch of it into your pocket? The quality of nothing hath not such need to hide itself. Let’s see. Come, if it be nothing I shall not need spectacles. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I beseech you, sir, pardon me. It is a letter from my brother that I have not all o’er-read; for so much as I have perused, I find it not fit for your liking. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Give me the letter, sir. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I shall offend either to detain or give it. The contents, as in part I understand them, are to blame. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let’s see, let’s see. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I [[Hope\|hope]] for my brother’s justification he wrote this but as an assay or taste of my virtue.<br>He gives [[Gloucester]] a letter |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (reads) ‘This policy of age makes the world bitter to the best of our times, keeps our fortunes from us till our oldness cannot relish them. I begin to find an idle and fond bondage in the oppression of aged tyranny, who sways not as it hath power but as it is suffered. Come to me, that of this I may speak more. If our father would [[Sleep\|sleep]] till I waked him, you should enjoy half his revenue for ever and live the beloved of your brother, [[Edgar]].’ Hum, conspiracy! ‘Slept till I waked him, you should enjoy half his revenue’—my son [[Edgar]]! Had he a hand to write this, a heart and brain to breed it in? When came this to you? Who brought it? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | It was not brought me, my lord, there’s the cunning of it. I found it thrown in at the casement of my closet. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | You know the character to be your brother’s? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | If the matter were good, my lord, I durst swear it were his; but in respect of that, I would fain think it were not. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | It is his. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | It is his hand, my lord, but I [[Hope\|hope]] his heart is not in the contents. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Hath he never heretofore sounded you in this business? 70 |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Never, my lord; but I have often heard him maintain it to be fit that, sons at perfect age and fathers declining, his father should be as ward to the son, and the son manage the revenue. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O [[Villain\|villain]], [[Villain\|villain]]—his very opinion in the tetter! Abhorred [[Villain\|villain]], unnatural, detested, brutish [[Villain\|villain]]—worse than brutish! Go, sir, seek him, ay, apprehend him. Abominable [[Villain\|villain]]! Where is he? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I do not [[Well\|well]] know, my lord. If it shall please you to suspend your indignation against my brother till you can derive from him better testimony of this intent, you should run a certain course; where if you violently proceed against him, mistaking his purpose, it would make a great gap in your own [[Honour\|honour]] and shake in pieces the heart of his obedience. I dare pawn down my life for him he hath wrote this to feel my affection to your [[Honour\|honour]], and to no further pretence of danger. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Think you so? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | If your [[Honour\|honour]] judge it meet, I will place you where you shall hear us confer of this, and by an auricular assurance have your satisfaction, and that without any further delay than this very evening. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He cannot be such a [[Monster\|monster]]. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Nor is pilot, sure. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | To his father, that so tenderly and entirely loves him—heaven and earth! [[Edmund]] seek him out, [[Wind\|wind]] me into him. I pray you, frame your business after your own wisdom. I would unstate myself to be in a due resolution. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I shall seek him, sir, presently, convey the business as I shall see means, and acquaint you withal. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | These late eclipses in the sun and [[Moon\|moon]] portend no good to us. Though the wisdom of [[Nature\|nature]] can reason thus and thus, yet [[Nature\|nature]] finds itself scourged by the sequent effects. [[Love]] cools, [[Friendship\|friendship]] falls off, brothers divide; in cities mutinies, in countries discords, palaces treason, the bond cracked between son and father. Find out this [[Villain\|villain]], [[Edmund]]; it shall lose thee nothing. Do it carefully. And the noble and true-hearted [[Kent]] banished, his offence honesty! Strange, strange! Exit |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | This is the excellent foppery of the world: that when we are sick in fortune—often the surfeit of our own behaviour—we make guilty of our disasters the sun, the [[Moon\|moon]], and the [[Stars\|stars]], as if we were villains by necessity, fools by heavenly compulsion, knaves, thieves, and treacherers by spherical predominance, drunkards, liars, and adulterers by an enforced obedience of planetary influence, and all that we are evil in by a divine thrusting on. An admirable evasion of whoremaster man, to lay his goatish disposition to the charge of [[Stars\|stars]]! My father compounded with my mother under the Dragon’s tail and my nativity was under Ursa Major, so that it follows I am rough and lecherous. Fut! I should have been that I am had the maidenliest star of the firmament twinkled on my bastardy. [[Edgar]] ... |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | and on’s cue out he comes, like the catastrophe of the old [[Comedy\|comedy]]; mine is villainous [[Melancholy\|melancholy]], with a sigh like them of Bedlam.—O, these eclipses do portend these divisions. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | How now, brother [[Edmund]], what serious contemplation are you in? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I am thinking, brother, of a prediction I read this other day, what should follow these eclipses. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Do you busy yourself about that? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I promise you, the effects he writ of succeed unhappily, as of unnaturalness between the [[Child\|child]] and the parent, [[Death\|death]], dearth, dissolutions of ancient amities, divisions in state, menaces and maledictions against [[King\|king]] and nobles, needless diffidences, banishment of friends, dissipation of cohorts, nuptial breaches, and I know not what. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | How long have you been a sectary astronomical? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Come, come, when saw you my father last? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Why, the [[Night\|night]] gone by. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Spake you with him? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Two hours together. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Parted you in good terms? Found you no displeasure in him by word or countenance? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | None at all. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Bethink yourself wherein you may have offended him, and at my entreaty forbear his presence till some little [[Time\|time]] hath qualified the heat of his displeasure, which at this instant so rageth in him that with the mischief of your person it would scarce allay. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Some [[Villain\|villain]] hath done me wrong. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | That’s my [[Fear\|fear]], brother. I advise you to the best. Go armed. I am no honest man if there be any good meaning towards you. I have told you what I have seen and heard but faintly, nothing like the image and horror of it. Pray you, away. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Shall I hear from you anon? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I do serve you in this business. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | A credulous father, and a brother noble,<br>Whose [[Nature\|nature]] is so far from doing harms<br>That he suspects none; on whose foolish honesty<br>My practices ride easy. I see the business.<br>Let me, if not by birth, have lands by wit.<br>All with me’s meet that I can fashion fit. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Sc. 3 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Gonoril and Oswald, her gentleman |
| GONORIL | Did my father strike my gentleman<br>For chiding of his [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| OSWALD | Yes, madam. |
| GONORIL | By day and [[Night\|night]] he wrongs me. Every hour<br>He flashes into one gross crime or other<br>That sets us all at odds. I’ll not endure it.<br>His knights grow riotous, and himself upbraids us<br>On every trifle. When he returns from hunting<br>I will not speak with him. Say I am sick.<br>If you come slack of former services<br>You shall do [[Well\|well]]; the fault of it I’ll answer. |
| *(didascalia)* | Hunting horns within |
| OSWALD | He’s coming, madam. I hear him. |
| GONORIL | Put on what weary negligence you please,<br>You and your fellow servants. I’d have it come in<br>question.<br>If he dislike it, let him to our sister,<br>Whose mind and mine I know in that are one,<br>Not to be overruled. Idle old man,<br>That still would manage those authorities<br>That he hath given away! Now, by my life,<br>Old fools are babes again, and must be used<br>With checks as flatteries, when they are seen abused.<br>Remember what I tell you. |
| OSWALD | Very [[Well\|well]], madam. |
| GONORIL | And let his knights have colder looks among you.<br>What grows of it, no matter. Advise your fellows so.<br>I would breed from hence occasions, and I shall,<br>That I may speak. I’ll write straight to my sister<br>To hold my very course. Go prepare for dinner. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |
| GONORIL | Sc. 4 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], disguised |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | If but as [[Well\|well]] I other accents borrow<br>That can my speech diffuse, my good intent<br>May carry through itself to that full issue<br>For which I razed my likeness. Now, banished [[Kent]],<br>If thou canst serve where thou dost stand condemned,<br>Thy master, whom thou lov’st, shall find thee full of<br>labour. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] and servants from hunting |
| LEAR | Let me not stay a jot for dinner. Go get it ready. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one |
| LEAR | (To [[Kent]]) How now, what art thou? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A man, sir. |
| LEAR | What dost thou profess? What wouldst thou with us? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I do profess to be no less than I seem, to serve him truly that will put me in trust, to [[Love\|love]] him that is honest, to converse with him that is wise and says little, to [[Fear\|fear]] judgement, to fight when I cannot choose, and to eat no fish. |
| LEAR | What art thou? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A very honest-hearted fellow, and as poor as the [[King]]. |
| LEAR | If thou be as poor for a subject as he is for a [[King\|king]], thou’rt poor enough. What wouldst thou? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Service. |
| LEAR | Who wouldst thou serve? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | You. |
| LEAR | Dost thou know me, fellow? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, sir, but you have that in your countenance which I would fain call master. |
| LEAR | What’s that? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Authority. |
| LEAR | What services canst do? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I can keep honest counsel, ride, run, mar a curious tale in telling it, and deliver a plain message bluntly. That which ordinary men are fit for I am qualified in; and the best of me is diligence. |
| LEAR | How old art thou? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Not so young to [[Love\|love]] a woman for singing, nor so old to dote on her for anything. I have years on my back forty-eight. |
| LEAR | Follow me. Thou shalt serve me, if I like thee no worse after dinner. I will not part from thee yet.—Dinner, ho, dinner! Where’s my knave, my [[Fool\|fool]]? Go you and call my [[Fool\|fool]] hither. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| LEAR | You, sirrah, where’s my daughter? |
| OSWALD | So please you— |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | What says the fellow there? Call the clotpoll back. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Servant and [[Kent]] |
| LEAR | Where’s my [[Fool\|fool]]? Ho, I think the world’s asleep. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] and a Servant |
| LEAR | How now, where’s that mongrel? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | He says, my lord, your daughter is not [[Well\|well]]. |
| LEAR | Why came not the slave back to me when I called him? |
| SERVANT | Sir, he answered me in the roundest manner he would not. |
| LEAR | A would not? |
| SERVANT | My lord, I know not what the matter is, but to my judgement your highness is not entertained with that ceremonious affection as you were wont. There’s a great abatement appears as [[Well\|well]] in the general dependants as in the Duke himself also, and your daughter. |
| LEAR | Ha, sayst thou so? |
| SERVANT | I beseech you pardon me, my lord, if I be mistaken, for my duty cannot be silent when I think your highness wronged. |
| LEAR | Thou but rememberest me of mine own conception. I have perceived a most faint neglect of late, which I have rather blamed as mine own jealous curiosity than as a very pretence and purport of unkindness. I will look further into’t. But where’s this [[Fool\|fool]]? I have not seen him these two days. |
| SERVANT | Since my young lady’s going into France, sir, the [[Fool\|fool]] hath much pined away. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR No more of that, I have noted it. Go you and tell my daughter I would speak with her. Exit one Go you, call hither my [[Fool\|fool]]. Exit one |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward crossing the stage |
| SERVANT | O you, sir, you, sir, come you hither. Who am I, sir? |
| OSWALD | My lady’s father. |
| LEAR | My lady’s father? My lord’s knave, you whoreson dog, you slave, you cur! |
| OSWALD | I am none of this, my lord, I beseech you pardon me. |
| LEAR | Do you bandy looks with me, you rascal? |
| *(didascalia)* | Lear strikes him |
| OSWALD | I’ll not be struck, my lord— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (tripping him) Nor tripped neither, you base football player. |
| LEAR | (to [[Kent]]) I thank thee, fellow. Thou serv’st me, and I’ll [[Love\|love]] thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Oswald) Come, sir, I’ll teach you differences. Away, away. If you will measure your lubber’s length again, tarry; but away if you have wisdom. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Oswald |
| LEAR | Now, friendly knave, I thank thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | There’s earnest of thy service.<br>He gives [[Kent]] money |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Let me hire him, too. (To [[Kent]]) Here’s my coxcomb. |
| LEAR | How now, my pretty knave, how dost thou? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | (to [[Kent]]) Sirrah, you were best take my coxcomb. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Why, for taking one’s part that’s out of favour. Nay, an thou canst not smile as the [[Wind\|wind]] sits, thou’lt catch cold shortly. There, take my coxcomb. Why, this fellow hath banished two on’s daughters and done the third a blessing against his will. If thou follow him, thou must needs wear my coxcomb. (To Lear) How now, nuncle? Would I had two coxcombs and two daughters. |
| LEAR | Why, my boy? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | If I gave them my living I’d keep my coxcombs myself. There’s mine; beg another off thy daughters. |
| LEAR | Take heed, sirrah—the whip. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | [[Truth]] is a dog that must to kennel. He must be whipped out when Lady the brach may stand by the [[Fire\|fire]] and stink. |
| LEAR | A pestilent gall to me! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] to [[Kent]] Sirrah, I’ll teach thee a speech. |
| LEAR | Do. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Mark it, uncle.<br>Have more than thou showest,<br>Speak less than thou knowest,<br>Lend less than thou owest,<br>Ride more than thou goest,<br>Learn more than thou trowest,<br>Set less than thou throwest,<br>Leave thy drink and thy whore,<br>And keep in-a-door,<br>And thou shalt have more<br>Than two tens to a score. |
| LEAR | This is nothing, [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Then, like the breath of an unfee’d lawyer, you gave me nothing for’t. Can you make no use of nothing, uncle? |
| LEAR | Why no, boy. Nothing can be made out of nothing. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | (to [[Kent]]) Prithee, tell him so much the rent of his land comes to. He will not believe a [[Fool\|fool]]. 130 |
| LEAR | A bitter [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Dost know the difference, my boy, between a bitter [[Fool\|fool]] and a sweet [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| LEAR | No, lad. Teach me. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] sings |
| LEAR | That lord that counselled thee To give away thy land,<br>Come, place him here by me;<br>Do thou for him stand.<br>The sweet and bitter [[Fool\|fool]]<br>Will presently appear,<br>The one in motley here,<br>The other found out there. |
| LEAR | Dost thou call me [[Fool\|fool]], boy? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | All thy other titles thou hast given away. That thou wast born with. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear) This is not altogether [[Fool\|fool]], my lord. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | No, [[Faith\|faith]]; lords and great men will not let me. If I had a monopoly out, they would have part on’t, and ladies too, they will not let me have all the [[Fool\|fool]] to myself—they’ll be snatching. Give me an egg, nuncle, and I’ll give thee two crowns. |
| LEAR | What two crowns shall they be? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Why, after I have cut the egg in the middle and eat up the meat, the two crowns of the egg. When thou clovest thy [[Crown\|crown]] i‘th’ middle and gavest away both parts, thou borest thy ass o’th’ back o’er the dirt. Thou hadst little wit in thy bald [[Crown\|crown]] when thou gavest thy golden one away. If I speak like myself in this, let him be whipped that first finds it so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Fools had ne’er less wit in a year,<br>For wise men are grown foppish.<br>They know not how their wits do wear,<br>Their manners are so apish. |
| LEAR | When were you wont to be so full of [[songs]], sirrah? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | I have used it, nuncle, ever since thou madest thy daughters thy mother; for when thou gavest them the rod and puttest down thine own breeches, |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Then they for sudden joy did weep, And I for sorrow sung,<br>That such a [[King\|king]] should play bo-peep<br>And go the fools among.<br>Prithee, nuncle, keep a schoolmaster that can teach thy [[Fool\|fool]] to lie. I would fain learn to lie. |
| LEAR | An you lie, we’ll have you whipped. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | I marvel what kin thou and thy daughters are. They’ll have me whipped for speaking true, thou wilt have me whipped for lying, and sometime I am whipped for holding my peace. I had rather be any kind of thing than a [[Fool\|fool]]; and yet I would not be thee, nuncle. Thou hast pared thy wit o’ both sides and left nothing in the middle. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Gonoril |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Here comes one of the parings. |
| LEAR | How now, daughter, what makes that frontlet on?<br>Methinks you are too much o’ late i’th’ frown. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] Thou wast a pretty fellow when thou hadst no need to care for her frown. Now thou art an O without a figure. I am better than thou art, now. I am a [[Fool\|fool]]; thou art nothing. To Gonoril Yes, forsooth, I will hold my tongue; so your face bids me, though you say nothing. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| LEAR | Mum, mum. He that keeps neither crust nor crumb,<br>Weary of all, shall want some.<br>That’s a shelled peascod. |
| GONORIL | (to Lear)<br>Not only, sir, this your all-licensed [[Fool\|fool]],<br>But other of your insolent retinue<br>Do hourly carp and quarrel, breaking forth<br>In rank and not-to-be-endured riots.<br>Sir, I had thought by making this [[Well\|well]] known unto<br>you<br>To have found a safe redress, but now grow fearful,<br>By what yourself too late have spoke and done,<br>That you protect this course, and put it on<br>By your allowance; which if you should, the fault<br>Would not scape censure, nor the redress [[Sleep\|sleep]]<br>Which in the tender of a wholesome weal<br>Might in their working do you that offence,<br>That else were shame, that then necessity<br>Must call discreet proceedings. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | (to Lear) For, you trow, nuncle, |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | The hedge-sparrow fed the cuckoo so long<br>That it had it head bit off by it young;<br>so out went the candle, and we were left darkling. |
| LEAR | (to Gonoril) Are you our daughter? |
| GONORIL | Come, sir, I would you would make use of that good<br>wisdom<br>Whereof I know you are fraught, and put away<br>These dispositions that of late transform you<br>From what you rightly are. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] May not an ass know when the cart draws the [[Horse\|horse]]? Sings ‘Whoop, jug, I [[Love\|love]] thee!’ |
| LEAR | Doth any here know me? Why, this is not Lear.<br>Doth Lear walk thus, speak thus? Where are his eyes?<br>Either his notion weakens, or his discernings<br>Are lethargied. Sleeping or waking, ha?<br>Sure, ’tis not so.<br>Who is it that can tell me who I am?<br>Lear’s [[Shadow\|shadow]]? I would learn that, for by the marks<br>Of sovereignty, knowledge, and reason<br>I should be false persuaded I had daughters. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Which they will make an obedient father. |
| LEAR | (to Gonoril)<br>Your name, fair gentlewoman? |
| GONORIL | Come, sir,<br>This admiration is much of the savour<br>Of other your new pranks. I do beseech you<br>Understand my purposes aright,<br>As you are old and reverend, should be wise.<br>Here do you keep a hundred knights and squires,<br>Men so disordered, so debauched and bold<br>That this our [[Court\|court]], infected with their manners,<br>Shows like a riotous inn, epicurism<br>And lust make more like to a tavern, or brothel,<br>Than a great palace. The shame itself doth speak<br>For instant remedy. Be thou desired,<br>By her that else will take the thing she begs,<br>A little to disquantity your train,<br>And the remainder that shall still depend<br>To be such men as may besort your age,<br>That know themselves and you. |
| LEAR | Darkness and devils!<br>Saddle my horses, call my train together!— |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| LEAR | Degenerate bastard, I’ll not trouble thee.<br>Yet have I left a daughter. |
| GONORIL | You strike my people, and your disordered rabble<br>Make servants of their betters. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Albany |
| LEAR | We that too late repent‘s—O sir, are you come?<br>Is it your will that we—prepare my horses. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| LEAR | Ingratitude, thou marble-hearted fiend,<br>More hideous when thou show’st thee in a [[Child\|child]]<br>Than the sea-[[Monster\|monster]]—(to Gonoril) detested kite, thou<br>liest.<br>My train are men of choice and rarest parts,<br>That all particulars of duty know,<br>And in the most exact regard support<br>The worships of their name. O most small fault,<br>How ugly didst thou in [[Cordelia]] show,<br>That, like an engine, wrenched my frame of [[Nature\|nature]]<br>From the fixed place, drew from my heart all [[Love\|love]],<br>And added to the gall! O Lear, Lear!<br>Beat at this gate that let thy folly in<br>And thy dear judgement out.—Go, go, my people! |
| ALBANY | My lord, I am guiltless as I am ignorant. |
| LEAR | It may be so, my lord. Hark, [[Nature\|nature]], hear:<br>Dear goddess, suspend thy purpose if<br>Thou didst intend to make this creature fruitful.<br>Into her womb convey sterility.<br>Dry up in her the organs of increase,<br>And from her derogate [[Body\|body]] never [[Spring\|spring]]<br>A babe to [[Honour\|honour]] her. If she must teem,<br>Create her [[Child\|child]] of spleen, that it may live<br>And be a thwart disnatured torment to her.<br>Let it stamp wrinkles in her brow of youth,<br>With cadent [[Tears\|tears]] fret channels in her cheeks,<br>Turn all her mother’s pains and benefits<br>To laughter and contempt, that she may feel—<br>That she may feel<br>How sharper than a serpent’s tooth it is<br>To have a thankless [[Child\|child]].—Go, go, my people! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear, [[Kent]], [[Fool]], and servants |
| ALBANY | Now, gods that we adore, whereof comes this? |
| GONORIL | Never afflict yourself to know the cause,<br>But let his disposition have that scope<br>That dotage gives it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] and his [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | What, fifty of my followers at a clap? |
| *(didascalia)* | Within a fortnight? |
| ALBANY | What is the matter, sir? |
| LEAR | I’ll tell thee. (To Gonoril) Life and [[Death\|death]]! I am<br>ashamed<br>That thou hast power to shake my manhood thus,<br>That these hot [[Tears\|tears]], that break from me perforce<br>And should make thee—worst blasts and fogs upon<br>thee!<br>Untented woundings of a father’s curse<br>Pierce every sense about thee! Old fond eyes,<br>Beweep this cause again I’ll pluck you out<br>And cast you, with the waters that you make,<br>To temper clay. Yea,<br>Is’t come to this? Yet have I left a daughter<br>Whom, I am sure, is kind and comfortable.<br>When she shall hear this of thee, with her nails<br>She’ll flay thy wolvish visage. Thou shalt find<br>That I’ll resume the shape which thou dost think<br>I have cast off for ever; thou shalt, I warrant thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| GONORIL | Do you mark that, my lord? |
| ALBANY | I cannot be so partial, Gonoril,<br>To the great [[Love\|love]] I bear you— |
| GONORIL | Come, sir, no more.—<br>You, more knave than [[Fool\|fool]], after your master! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Nuncle Lear, nuncle Lear, tarry, and take the [[Fool\|fool]] with thee.<br>A fox when one has caught her,<br>And such a daughter,<br>Should sure to the slaughter,<br>If my cap would buy a halter.<br>So, the [[Fool\|fool]] follows after. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| GONORIL | What, Oswald, ho! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald |
| OSWALD | Here, madam. |
| GONORIL | What, have you writ this letter to my sister? |
| OSWALD | Yes, madam. |
| GONORIL | Take you some company, and away to [[Horse\|horse]].<br>Inform her full of my particular fears,<br>And thereto add such reasons of your own<br>As may compact it more. Get you gone,<br>And after, your retinue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Oswald |
| GONORIL | Now, my lord,<br>This milky gentleness and course of yours,<br>Though I dislike not, yet under pardon<br>You’re much more ataxed for want of wisdom<br>Than praised for harmful mildness. |
| ALBANY | How far your eyes may pierce I cannot tell.<br>Striving to better aught, we mar what’s [[Well\|well]]. |
| GONORIL | Nay, then— |
| ALBANY | [[Well]], [[Well\|well]], the event. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 5 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR to [[Kent]] Go you before to [[Gloucester]] with these letters. Acquaint my daughter no further with anything you know than comes from her demand out of the letter. If your diligence be not speedy, I shall be there before you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I will not [[Sleep\|sleep]], my lord, till I have delivered your letter. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | If a man’s brains were in his heels, were’t not in danger of kibes? |
| LEAR | Ay, boy. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Then, I prithee, be merry: thy wit shall ne’er go slipshod. |
| LEAR | Ha, ha, ha! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Shalt see thy other daughter will use thee kindly, for though she’s as like this as a crab is like an apple, yet I con what I can tell. |
| LEAR | Why, what canst thou tell, my boy? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Why, to keep his eyes on either side ’s nose, that what a man cannot smell out, a may spy into. |
| LEAR | I did her wrong. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Canst tell how an oyster makes his shell? |
| LEAR | No. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Nor I neither; but I can tell why a snail has a [[house]]. |
| LEAR | Why? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Why, to put his head in, not to give it away to his daughter and leave his horns without a case. |
| LEAR | I will forget my [[Nature\|nature]]. So kind a father!<br>Be my horses ready? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Thy asses are gone about them. The reason why the seven [[Stars\|stars]] are no more than seven is a pretty reason. |
| LEAR | Because they are not eight. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Yes. Thou wouldst make a good [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| LEAR | To take’t again perforce—[[Monster\|monster]] ingratitude! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | If thou wert my [[Fool\|fool]], nuncle, I’d have thee beaten for being old before thy [[Time\|time]]. |
| LEAR | How’s that? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Thou shouldst not have been old before thou hadst been wise. |
| LEAR | O, let me not be mad, sweet heaven!<br>I would not be mad.<br>Keep me in temper. I would not be mad. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| LEAR | Are the horses ready? |
| SERVANT | Ready, my lord. |
| LEAR | (to [[Fool]]) Come, boy. Exeunt Lear and Servant |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | She that is maid now, and laughs at my departure,<br>Shall not be a maid long, except things be cut shorter. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Sc. 6 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edmund]] the bastard, and Curan, meeting |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Save thee, Curan. |
| CURAN | And you, sir. I have been with your father, and given him notice that the Duke of Cornwall and his duchess will be here with him tonight. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | How comes that? |
| CURAN | Nay, I know not. You have heard of the news abroad?—I mean the whispered ones, for there are yet but ear-bussing arguments. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Not. I pray you, what are they? |
| CURAN | Have you heard of no likely wars towards twixt the two Dukes of Cornwall and Albany? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Not a word. |
| CURAN | You may then in [[Time\|time]]. Fare you [[Well\|well]], sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | The Duke be here tonight! The better, best.<br>This weaves itself perforce into my business. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] at a [[Window\|window]] above |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | My father hath set guard to take my brother,<br>And I have one thing of a queasy question<br>Which must ask briefness. Wit and fortune help!—<br>Brother, a word. Descend, brother, I say. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] climbs down |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | My father watches. O, fly this place.<br>Intelligence is given where you are hid.<br>You have now the good advantage of the [[Night\|night]].<br>Have you not spoken ‘gainst the Duke of Cornwall<br>aught?<br>He’s coming hither now, in the [[Night\|night]], i’th’ haste,<br>And [[Regan]] with him. Have you nothing said<br>Upon his party against the Duke of Albany?<br>Advise you— |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I am sure on’t, not a word. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I hear my father coming. Pardon me.<br>In cunning I must draw my [[Sword\|sword]] upon you.<br>Seem to defend yourself. Now, quit you [[Well\|well]].<br>(Calling) Yield, come before my father. Light here,<br>here!<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Fly, brother, fly! (Calling) Torches, torches!<br>(To [[Edgar]]) So, farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Some [[Blood\|blood]] drawn on me would beget opinion<br>Of my more fierce endeavour.<br>He wounds his arm<br>I have seen<br>Drunkards do more than this in sport. (Calling) Father,<br>father!<br>Stop, stop! Ho, help! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] and others |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now, [[Edmund]], where is the [[Villain\|villain]]? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Here stood he in the dark, his sharp [[Sword\|sword]] out,<br>Warbling of wicked charms, conjuring the [[Moon\|moon]]<br>To stand ’s auspicious mistress. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But where is he? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Look, sir, I bleed. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Where is the [[Villain\|villain]], [[Edmund]]? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Fled this way, sir, when by no means he could— |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Pursue him, go after. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt others |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By no means what? |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Persuade me to the murder of your lordship,<br>But that I told him the revengive gods<br>’Gainst parricides did all their thunders bend,<br>Spoke with how manifold and strong a bond<br>The [[Child\|child]] was bound to the father. Sir, in fine,<br>Seeing how loathly opposite I stood<br>To his unnatural purpose, with fell motion,<br>With his prepared [[Sword\|sword]] he charges [[Home\|home]]<br>My unprovided [[Body\|body]], lanced mine arm;<br>But when he saw my best alarumed spirits<br>Bold in the quarrel’s rights, roused to the encounter,<br>Or whether ghasted by the noise I made |
| *(didascalia)* | Or   I know not, |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | But suddenly he fled. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let him fly far,<br>Not in this land shall he remain uncaught,<br>And found, dispatch. The noble Duke my master,<br>My worthy arch and patron, comes tonight.<br><br>By his authority I will proclaim it<br>That he which finds him shall deserve our thanks,<br>Bringing the murderous caitiff to the stake;<br>He that conceals him, [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | When I dissuaded him from his intent<br>And found him pitched to do it, with curst speech<br>I threatened to discover him. He replied,<br>‘Thou unpossessing bastard, dost thou think<br>If I would stand against thee, could the reposure<br>Of any trust, virtue, or worth in thee<br>Make thy words faithed? No, what I should deny—<br>As this I would, ay, though thou didst produce<br>My very character—I’d turn it all<br>To thy suggestion, plot, and damned pretence,<br>And thou must make a dullard of the world<br>If they not thought the profits of my [[Death\|death]]<br>Were very pregnant and potential spurs<br>To make thee seek it.’ |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Strong and fastened [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>Would he deny his letter? I never got him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Trumpets within |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Hark, the Duke’s trumpets. I know not why he comes.<br>All ports I’ll bar. The [[Villain\|villain]] shall not scape.<br>The Duke must grant me that; besides, his picture<br>I will send far and near, that all the kingdom<br>May have note of him—and of my land,<br>Loyal and natural boy, I’ll work the means<br>To make thee capable. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | of<br>Cornwall and [[Regan]] |
| CORNWALL | How now, my noble friend? Since I came hither,<br>Which I can call but now, I have heard strange news. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | If it be true, all vengeance comes too short<br>Which can pursue the offender. How dost, my lord? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Madam, my old heart is cracked, is cracked. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What, did my father’s godson seek your life?<br>He whom my father named, your [[Edgar]]? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, lady, lady; shame would have it hid. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Was he not companion with the riotous knights<br>That tend upon my father? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I know not, madam. ’Tis too bad, too bad. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Yes, madam, he was. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | No marvel, then, though he were ill affected.<br>’Tis they have put him on the old man’s [[Death\|death]],<br>To have the spoil and waste of his revenues.<br>I have this present evening from my sister<br>Been [[Well\|well]] informed of them, and with such cautions<br>That if they come to sojourn at my [[house]]<br>I’ll not be there. |
| CORNWALL | Nor I, assure thee, [[Regan]].<br>[[Edmund]], I heard that you have shown your father<br>A childlike office. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | ’Twas my duty, sir. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Cornwall)<br>He did betray his practice, and received<br>This hurt you see striving to apprehend him. |
| CORNWALL | Is he pursued? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| CORNWALL | If he be taken, he shall never more<br>Be feared of doing harm. Make your own purpose<br>How in my strength you please. For you, [[Edmund]],<br>Whose virtue and obedience doth this instant<br>So much commend itself, you shall be ours.<br>Natures of such deep trust we shall much need.<br>You we first seize on. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | I shall serve you truly,<br>However else. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Cornwall) For him I thank your grace. |
| CORNWALL | You know not why we came to visit you— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | This out-of-season threat’ning dark-eyed [[Night\|night]]-<br>Occasions, noble [[Gloucester]], of some poise,<br>Wherein we must have use of your advice.<br>Our father he hath writ, so hath our sister,<br>Of differences which I least thought it fit<br>To answer from our [[Home\|home]]. The several messengers<br>From hence attend dispatch. Our good old friend,<br>Lay comforts to your bosom, and bestow<br>Your needful counsel to our business,<br>Which craves the instant use. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I serve you, madam.<br>Your graces are right welcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sc. 7 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], disguised, at one door, and Oswald the steward, at another door |
| OSWALD | Good even to thee, friend. Art of the [[house]]? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ay. |
| OSWALD | Where may we set our horses? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’th’ mire. |
| OSWALD | Prithee, if thou [[Love\|love]] me, tell me. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I [[Love\|love]] thee not. |
| OSWALD | Why then, I care not for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | If I had thee in Lipsbury pinfold I would make thee care for me. |
| OSWALD | Why dost thou use me thus? I know thee not. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fellow, I know thee. |
| OSWALD | What dost thou know me for? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A knave, a rascal, an eater of broken meats, a base, proud, shallow, beggarly, three-suited, hundred-pound, filthy worsted-stocking knave; a lily-livered, action-taking knave; a whoreson, glass-gazing, superfinical rogue; one-trunk-inheriting slave; one that wouldst be a bawd in way of good service, and art nothing but the composition of a knave, beggar, coward, pander, and the son and heir of a mongrel bitch, whom I will beat into clamorous whining if thou deny the least syllable of the addition. |
| OSWALD | What a monstrous fellow art thou, thus to rail on one that’s neither known of thee nor knows thee! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What a brazen-faced varlet art thou, to deny thou knowest me! Is it two days ago since I beat thee and tripped up thy heels before the [[King]]? Draw, you rogue; for though it be [[Night\|night]], the [[Moon\|moon]] shines. |
| *(didascalia)* | He draws his [[Sword\|sword]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’ll make a sop of the moonshine o’ you. Draw, you whoreson, cullionly barber-monger, draw! |
| OSWALD | Away. I have nothing to do with thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Draw, you rascal. You bring letters against the [[King]], and take [[Vanity]] the puppet’s part against the royalty of her father. Draw, you rogue, or I’ll so carbonado your shanks—draw, you rascal, come your ways! |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, help! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Strike, you slave! Stand, rogue! Stand, you neat slave, strike! |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, help! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edmund]] the bastard with his rapier drawn, then the [[Duke of Gloucester]], then the Duke of Cornwall and [[Regan]] the Duchess |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] parting them How now, what’s the matter? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With you, goodman boy. An you please come, I’ll flesh you. Come on, young master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Weapons? Arms? What’s the matter here? |
| CORNWALL | Keep peace, upon your lives. He dies that strikes again. What’s the matter? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | The messengers from our sister and the [[King]]. |
| CORNWALL | (to [[Kent]] and Oswald) What’s your difference? Speak. |
| OSWALD | I am scarce in breath, my lord. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No marvel, you have so bestirred your valour, you cowardly rascal. [[Nature]] disclaims in thee; a tailor made thee. |
| CORNWALL | Thou art a strange fellow—a tailor make a man? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ay, a tailor, sir. A stone-cutter or a painter could not have made him so ill though he had been but two hours at the trade. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Speak yet; how grew your quarrel? |
| OSWALD | This ancient ruffian, sir, whose life I have spared at suit of his grey beard— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Thou whoreson Z, thou unnecessary letter—(to Cornwall) my lord, if you’ll give me leave I will tread this unboulted [[Villain\|villain]] into mortar and daub the walls of a jakes with him. (To Oswald) Spare my grey beard, you wagtail? |
| CORNWALL | Peace, sir. You beastly knave, have you no reverence? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Yes, sir, but anger has a privilege. |
| CORNWALL | Why art thou angry? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | That such a slave as this should wear a [[Sword\|sword]],<br>That wears no honesty. Such smiling rogues<br>As these, like rats, oft bite those cords in twain<br>Which are too entrenched to unloose, smooth every<br>passion<br>That in the natures of their lords [[Rebel\|rebel]],<br>Bring oil to [[Fire\|fire]], [[Snow\|snow]] to their colder moods,<br>Renege, affirm, and turn their halcyon beaks<br>With every gale and vary of their masters,<br>Knowing naught, like [[dogs]], but following.<br>(To Oswald) A plague upon your epileptic visage!<br>Smile you my speeches as I were a [[Fool\|fool]]?<br>Goose, an I had you upon Sarum Plain<br>I’d send you cackling [[Home\|home]] to Camelot. |
| CORNWALL | What, art thou mad, old fellow? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] to [[Kent]] How fell you out? Say that. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No contraries hold more antipathy<br>Than I and such a knave. |
| CORNWALL | Why dost thou call him knave?<br>What’s his offence? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | His countenance likes me not. |
| CORNWALL | No more perchance does mine, or his, or hers. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, ’tis my occupation to be plain:<br>I have seen better faces in my [[Time\|time]]<br>Than stands on any shoulder that I see<br>Before me at this instant. |
| CORNWALL | This is a fellow<br>Who, having been praised for bluntness, doth affect<br>A saucy roughness, and constrains the garb<br>Quite from his [[Nature\|nature]]. He cannot flatter, he.<br>He must be plain, he must speak [[Truth\|truth]].<br>An they will take’t, so; if not, he’s plain.<br>These kind of knaves I know, which in this plainness<br>Harbour more craft and more corrupter ends<br>Than twenty silly-ducking observants<br>That stretch their duties nicely. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, in good sooth, or in sincere verity,<br>Under the allowance of your grand aspect,<br>Whose influence, like the wreath of radiant [[Fire\|fire]]<br>In flickering Phoebus’ front— |
| CORNWALL | What mean’st thou by this? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | To go out of my dialect, which you discommend so much. I know, sir, I am no flatterer. He that beguiled you in a plain accent was a plain knave, which for my part I will not be, though I should win your displeasure to entreat me to’t. |
| CORNWALL | (to Oswald)<br>What’s the offence you gave him? |
| OSWALD | I never gave him any.<br>It pleased the [[King]] his master very late<br>To strike at me upon his misconstruction,<br>When he, conjunct, and flattering his displeasure,<br>Tripped me behind; being down, insulted, railed,<br>And put upon him such a deal of man that<br>That worthied him, got praises of the [[King]]<br>For him attempting who was [[Self\|self]]-subdued,<br>And in the fleshment of this dread exploit<br>Drew on me here again. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None of these rogues and cowards<br>But [[Ajax]] is their [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | CORNWALL calling Bring forth the stocks, ho!— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | You stubborn, ancient knave, you reverend braggart,<br>We’ll teach you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I am too old to learn.<br>Call not your stocks for me. I serve the [[King]],<br>On whose employments I was sent to you.<br>You should do small respect, show too bold malice<br>Against the grace and person of my master,<br>Stocking his messenger. |
| *(didascalia)* | CORNWALL calling Fetch forth the stocks!— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | As I have life and [[Honour\|honour]], there shall he sit till [[Noon\|noon]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Till [[Noon\|noon]]?—till [[Night\|night]], my lord, and all [[Night\|night]] too. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, madam, if I were your father’s dog<br>You could not use me so. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sir, being his knave, I will. |
| *(didascalia)* | Stocks brought out |
| CORNWALL | This is a fellow of the selfsame [[Nature\|nature]]<br>Our sister speaks of.—Come, bring away the stocks. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let me beseech your grace not to do so.<br>His fault is much, and the good [[King]] his master<br>Will check him for’t. Your purposed low correction<br>Is such as basest and contemnèd wretches<br>For pilf’rings and most common trespasses<br>Are punished with. The [[King]] must take it ill<br>That he’s so slightly valued in his messenger,<br>Should have him thus restrained. |
| CORNWALL | I’ll answer that. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | My sister may receive it much more worse<br>To have her gentlemen abused, assaulted,<br>For following her affairs. Put in his legs.<br>They put [[Kent]] in the stocks<br>Come, my good lord, away! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Gloucester]] and [[Kent]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I am sorry for thee, friend. ’Tis the Duke’s pleasure,<br>Whose disposition, all the world [[Well\|well]] knows,<br>Will not be rubbed nor stopped. I’ll entreat for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Pray you, do not, sir. I have watched and travelled<br>hard.<br>Some [[Time\|time]] I shall [[Sleep\|sleep]] out; the rest I’ll whistle.<br>A good man’s fortune may grow out at heels.<br>Give you good morrow. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The Duke’s to blame in this; ’twill be ill took. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good [[King]], that must approve the common say:<br>Thou out of heaven’s benediction com’st<br>To the warm sun. |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes out a letter |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Approach, thou beacon to this under globe,<br>That by thy comfortable beams I may<br>Peruse this letter. Nothing almost sees miracles<br>But misery. I know ’tis from [[Cordelia]],<br>Who hath now fortunately been informed<br>Of my obscured course, and shall find [[Time\|time]]<br>For this enormous state, seeking to give<br>Losses their remedies. All weary and overwatched,<br>Take vantage, heavy eyes, not to behold<br>This shameful lodging. Fortune, good [[Night\|night]];<br>Smile; once more turn thy [[Wheel\|wheel]].<br>He sleeps |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I heard myself proclaimed,<br>And by the happy hollow of a tree<br>Escaped the hunt. No port is free, no place<br>That guard and most unusual vigilance<br>Does not attend my taking. While I may scape<br>I will preserve myself, and am bethought<br>To take the basest and most poorest shape<br>That ever penury in contempt of man<br>Brought near to beast. My face I’ll grime with filth,<br>Blanket my loins, elf all my hair with knots,<br>And with presented [[Nakedness\|nakedness]] outface<br>The [[Wind\|wind]] and persecution of the sky.<br>The country gives me proof and precedent<br>Of Bedlam beggars who with roaring voices<br>Strike in their numbed and mortified bare arms<br>Pins, wooden pricks, nails, sprigs of rosemary,<br>And with this horrible object from low farms,<br>Poor pelting villages, sheep-cotes and mills<br>Sometime with lunatic bans, sometime with prayers<br>Enforce their [[Charity\|charity]]. ‘Poor Tuelygod, Poor Tom!’<br>That’s something yet. [[Edgar]] I nothing am. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], his [[Fool]], and a Knight |
| LEAR | ’Tis strange that they should so depart from [[Home\|home]]<br>And not send back my messenger. |
| KNIGHT | As I learned,<br>The [[Night\|night]] before there was no purpose<br>Of his remove. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (waking) Hail to thee, noble master. |
| LEAR | How! Mak’st thou this shame thy pastime? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Ha, ha, look, he wears cruel garters! Horses are tied by the heads, [[dogs]] and bears by th’ neck, monkeys by th’ loins, and men by th’ legs. When a man’s over-lusty at legs, then he wears wooden nether-stocks. |
| LEAR | (to [[Kent]])<br>What’s he that hath so much thy place mistook<br>To set thee here? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | It is both he and she:<br>Your son and daughter. |
| LEAR | No. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Yes. |
| LEAR | No, I say. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I say yea. |
| LEAR | No, no, they would not. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Yes, they have. |
| LEAR | By Jupiter, I swear no. They durst not do‘t,<br>They would not, could not do’t. ’Tis worse than murder,<br>To do upon respect such violent outrage.<br>Resolve me with all modest haste which way<br>Thou mayst deserve or they propose this usage,<br>Coming from us. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My lord, when at their [[Home\|home]]<br>I did commend your highness’ letters to them,<br>Ere I was risen from the place that showed<br>My duty kneeling, came there a reeking post<br>Stewed in his haste, half breathless, panting forth<br>From Gonoril, his mistress, salutations,<br>Delivered letters spite of intermission,<br>Which presently they read, on whose contents<br>They summoned up their meiny, straight took [[Horse\|horse]],<br>Commanded me to follow and attend<br>The leisure of their answer, gave me cold looks;<br>And meeting here the other messenger,<br>Whose welcome I perceived had poisoned mine—<br>Being the very fellow that of late<br>Displayed so saucily against your highness—<br>Having more man than wit about me, drew.<br>He raised the [[house]] with loud and coward cries.<br>Your son and daughter found this trespass worth<br>This shame which here it suffers. |
| LEAR | O, how this mother swells up toward my heart!<br>Histerica passio, down, thou climbing sorrow;<br>Thy element’s betow.—Where is this daughter? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With the Earl, sir, within. |
| LEAR | Follow me not; stay there. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| KNIGHT | (to [[Kent]])<br>Made you no more offence than what you speak of? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No. How chance the [[King]] comes with so small a train? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | An thou hadst been set in the stocks for that question, thou hadst [[Well\|well]] deserved it. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | We’ll set thee to school to an ant, to teach thee there’s no labouring in the [[Winter\|winter]]. All that follow their noses are led by their eyes but blind men, and there’s not a nose among a hundred but can smell him that’s stinking. Let go thy hold when a great [[Wheel\|wheel]] runs down a [[Hill\|hill]], lest it break thy neck with following it; but the great one that goes up the [[Hill\|hill]], let him draw thee after. When a wise man gives thee better counsel, give me mine again. I would have none but knaves follow it, since a [[Fool\|fool]] gives it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | That sir that serves for gain And follows but for form,<br>Will pack when it begin to rain,<br>And leave thee in the [[Storm\|storm]].<br><br>But I will tarry, the [[Fool\|fool]] will stay,<br>And let the wise man fly.<br>The knave turns [[Fool\|fool]] that runs away,<br>The [[Fool\|fool]] no knave, pardie. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Where learnt you this, [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Not in the stocks. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] and the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| LEAR | Deny to speak with me? They’re sick, they’re weary?<br>They travelled hard tonight?—mere insolence,<br>Ay, the images of revolt and flying off.<br>Fetch me a better answer. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | My dear lord,<br>You know the fiery quality of the Duke,<br>How unremovable and fixed he is<br>In his own course. |
| LEAR | Vengeance, [[Death\|death]], plague, confusion! What ‘fiery quality’? Why, [[Gloucester]], [[Gloucester]], I’d Speak with the Duke of Cornwall and his wife. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| LEAR | The [[King]] would speak with Cornwall; the dear father<br>Would with his daughter speak, commands, tends<br>service.<br>‘Fiery’? The Duke?—tell the hot Duke that Lear—<br>No, but not yet. Maybe he is not [[Well\|well]].<br>Infirmity doth still neglect all office<br>Whereto our health is bound. We are not ourselves<br>When [[Nature\|nature]], being oppressed, commands the mind<br>To suffer with the [[Body\|body]]. I’ll forbear,<br>And am fallen out with my more headier will,<br>To take the indisposed and sickly fit<br>For the sound man.—[[Death]] on my state,<br>Wherefore should he sit here? This act persuades me<br>That this remotion of the Duke and her<br>Is practice only. Give me my servant forth.<br>Tell the Duke and ’s wife I’ll speak with them,<br>Now, presently. Bid them come forth and hear me,<br>Or at their chamber door I’ll beat the drum<br>Till it cry [[Sleep\|sleep]] to [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I would have all [[Well\|well]]<br>Betwixt you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | O, my heart, my heart! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Cry to it, nuncle, as the cockney did to the eels when she put ‘em i’th’ paste alive. She rapped ‘em o’th’ coxcombs with a stick, and cried ‘Down, wantons, down!’ ’Twas her brother that, in pure kindness to his [[Horse\|horse]], buttered his hay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall and [[Regan]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and others |
| LEAR | Good morrow to you both. |
| CORNWALL | Hail to your grace. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent]] here set at liberty |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I am glad to see your highness. |
| LEAR | [[Regan]], I think you are. I know what reason<br>I have to think so. If thou shouldst not be glad<br>I would [[divorce]] me from thy mother’s shrine,<br>Sepulchring an adultress. (To [[Kent]]) Yea, are you free?<br>Some other [[Time\|time]] for that.—Belovèd [[Regan]],<br>Thy sister is naught. O, [[Regan]], she hath tied<br>Sharp-toothed unkindness like a vulture here.<br>I can scarce speak to thee. Thou’lt not believe<br>Of how deplored a quality—O, [[Regan]]! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, sir, take patience. I have [[Hope\|hope]]<br>You less know how to value her [[Desert\|desert]]<br>Than she to slack her duty. |
| LEAR | My curses on her. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, you are old.<br>[[Nature]] in you stands on the very verge<br>Of her confine. You should be ruled and led<br>By some discretion that discerns your state<br>Better than you yourself. Therefore I pray<br>That to our sister you do make return;<br>Say you have wronged her, sir. |
| LEAR | Ask her [[Forgiveness\|forgiveness]]?<br>Do you mark how this becomes the [[house]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Kneeling ‘Dear daughter, I confess that I am old. |
| LEAR | Age is unnecessary. On my knees I beg<br>That you’ll vouchsafe me raiment, bed, and food.’ |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, no more. These are unsightly tricks.<br>Return you to my sister. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR rising No, [[Regan]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | She hath abated me of half my train,<br>Looked black upon me, struck me with her tongue<br>Most serpent-like upon the very heart.<br>All the stored vengeances of heaven fall<br>On her ungrateful top! Strike her young bones,<br>You taking airs, with lameness! |
| CORNWALL | Fie, fie, sir. |
| LEAR | You nimble lightnings, dart your blinding flames<br>Into her scornful eyes. Infect her [[Beauty\|beauty]],<br>You fen-sucked fogs drawn by the pow’rful sun<br>To fall and blast her [[Pride\|pride]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O, the blest gods!<br>So will you wish on me when the rash mood— |
| LEAR | No, [[Regan]]. Thou shalt never have my curse.<br>Thy tender-hested [[Nature\|nature]] shall not give<br>Thee o‘er to harshness. Her eyes are fierce, but thine<br>Do comfort and not burn. ’Tis not in thee<br>To grudge my pleasures, to cut off my train,<br>To bandy hasty words, to scant my sizes,<br>And, in conclusion, to oppose the bolt<br>Against my coming in. Thou better know’st<br>The offices of [[Nature\|nature]], bond of [[Childhood\|childhood]],<br>Effects of courtesy, dues of gratitude.<br>Thy half of the kingdom hast thou not forgot,<br>Wherein I thee endowed. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, to th’ purpose. |
| LEAR | Who put my man i’th’ stocks? |
| *(didascalia)* | Trumpets within |
| CORNWALL | What trumpet’s that? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I know’t, my sister’s. This approves her letters<br>That she would soon be here. (To Oswald) Is your lady<br>come? |
| LEAR | This is a slave whose easy-borrowed [[Pride\|pride]]<br>Dwells in the fickle grace of her a follows. |
| *(didascalia)* | He strikes Oswald |
| LEAR | Out, varlet, from my sight! |
| CORNWALL | What means your grace? |
