| *(didascalia)* | Enter Gonoril |
| GONORIL | Who struck my servant? [[Regan]], I have good [[Hope\|hope]]<br>Thou didst not know on’t. |
| LEAR | Who comes here? O heavens,<br>If you do [[Love\|love]] old men, if your sweet sway<br>Allow obedience, if yourselves are old,<br>Make it your cause! Send down and take my part.<br>(To Gonoril) Art not ashamed to look upon this<br>beard?<br>O [[Regan]], wilt thou take her by the hand? |
| GONORIL | Why not by the hand, sir? How have I offended?<br>All’s not offence that indiscretion finds<br>And dotage terms so. |
| LEAR | O sides, you are too tough!<br>Will you yet hold?—How came my man i’th’ stocks? |
| CORNWALL | I set him there, sir; but his own disorders<br>Deserved much less advancement. |
| LEAR | You? Did you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, father, being weak, seem so.<br>If till the expiration of your month<br>You will return and sojourn with my sister,<br>Dismissing half your train, come then to me.<br>I am now from [[Home\|home]], and out of that provision<br>Which shall be needful for your entertainment. |
| LEAR | Return to her, and fifty men dismissed?<br>No, rather I abjure all roofs, and choose<br>To be a comrade with the wolf and owl,<br>To wage against the enmity of the air<br>Necessity’s sharp pinch. Return with her?<br>Why, the hot-[[Blood\|blood]] in France that dowerless took<br>Our youngest born—I could as [[Well\|well]] be brought<br>To knee his throne and, squire-like, pension beg<br>To keep base life afoot. Return with her?<br>Persuade me rather to be slave and sumpter<br>To this detested groom. |
| GONORIL | At your choice, sir. |
| LEAR | Now I prithee, daughter, do not make me mad.<br>I will not trouble thee, my [[Child\|child]]. Farewell.<br>We’ll no more meet, no more see one another.<br>But yet thou art my flesh, my [[Blood\|blood]], my daughter—<br>Or rather a disease that lies within my flesh,<br>Which I must needs call mine. Thou art a boil,<br>A plague-sore, an embossed carbuncle<br>In my corrupted [[Blood\|blood]]. But I’ll not chide thee.<br>Let shame come when it will, I do not call it.<br>I do not bid the thunder-bearer shoot,<br>Nor tell tales of thee to high-judging Jove.<br>Mend when thou canst; be better at thy leisure.<br>I can be patient, I can stay with [[Regan]],<br>I and my hundred knights. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Not altogether so, sir.<br>I look not for you yet, nor am provided<br>For your fit welcome. Give ear, sir, to my sister;<br>For those that mingle reason with your passion<br>Must be content to think you are old, and so—<br>But she knows what she does. |
| LEAR | Is this [[Well\|well]] spoken now? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I dare avouch it, sir. What, fifty followers?<br>Is it not [[Well\|well]]? What should you need of more,<br>Yea, or so many, sith that both charge and danger<br>Speaks ‘gainst so great a number? How in a [[house]]<br>Should many people under two commands<br>Hold amity? ’Tis hard, almost impossible. |
| GONORIL | Why might not you, my lord, receive attendance<br>From those that she calls servants, or from mine? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why not, my lord? If then they chanced to slack you,<br>We could control them. If you will come to me—<br>For now I spy a danger—I entreat you<br>To bring but five-and-twenty; to no more<br>Will I give place or notice. |
| LEAR | I gave you all. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And in good [[Time\|time]] you gave it. |
| LEAR | Made you my guardians, my depositaries,<br>But kept a reservation to be followed<br>With such a number. What, must I come to you<br>With five-and-twenty, [[Regan]]? Said you so? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And speak’t again, my lord. No more with me. |
| LEAR | Those wicked creatures yet do seem [[Well\|well]] favoured<br>When others are more wicked. Not being the worst<br>Stands in some rank of praise. (To Gonoril) I’ll go with<br>thee.<br>Thy fifty yet doth [[Double\|double]] five-and-twenty,<br>And thou art twice her [[Love\|love]]. |
| GONORIL | Hear me, my lord.<br>What need you five-and-twenty, ten, or five,<br>To follow in a [[house]] where twice so many<br>Have a command to tend you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What needs one? |
| LEAR | O, reason not the need! Our basest beggars<br>Are in the poorest thing superfluous.<br>Allow not [[Nature\|nature]] more than [[Nature\|nature]] needs,<br>Man’s life is cheap as beast’s. Thou art a lady.<br>If only to go warm were gorgeous,<br>Why, [[Nature\|nature]] needs not what thou, gorgeous, wearest,<br>Which scarcely keeps thee warm. But for true need—<br>You heavens, give me that patience, patience I need.<br>You see me here, you gods, a poor old fellow,<br>As full of grief as age, wretched in both.<br>If it be you that stirs these daughters’ hearts<br>Against their father, [[Fool\|fool]] me not so much<br>To bear it tamely. Touch me with noble anger.<br>O, let not women’s weapons, [[Water\|water]]-drops,<br>Stain my man’s cheeks! No, you unnatural hags,<br>I will have such revenges on you both<br>That all the world shall—I will do such things—<br>What they are, yet I know not; but they shall be<br>The terrors of the earth. You think I’ll weep.<br>No, I’ll not weep. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] within |
| LEAR | I have full cause of weeping, but this heart<br>Shall break into a hundred thousand flaws<br>Or ere I’ll weep.—O [[Fool\|fool]], I shall go mad! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear, [[Gloucester]], [[Kent]], Knight, and [[Fool]] |
| CORNWALL | Let us withdraw. ’Twill be a [[Storm\|storm]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | This [[house]] is little. The old man and his people<br>Cannot be [[Well\|well]] bestowed. |
| GONORIL | ’Tis his own blame;<br>Hath put himself from rest, and must needs taste his folly. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | For his particular I’ll receive him gladly,<br>But not one follower. |
| CORNWALL | So am I purposed. Where is my lord of [[Gloucester]]? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Followed the old man forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | He is returned. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is in high rage, and will I know not whither. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | ’Tis good to give him way. He leads himself. |
| GONORIL | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>My lord, entreat him by no means to stay. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, the [[Night\|night]] comes on, and the bleak winds<br>Do sorely rustle. For many miles about<br>There’s not a bush. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, to wilful men<br>The injuries that they themselves procure<br>Must be their schoolmasters. Shut up your doors.<br>He is attended with a desperate train,<br>And what they may incense him to, being apt<br>To have his ear abused, wisdom bids [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| CORNWALL | Shut up your doors, my lord. ‘Tis a wild [[Night\|night]].<br>My [[Regan]] counsels [[Well\|well]]. Come out o’th’ [[Storm\|storm]]. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 8 |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]]. Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and First Gentleman, at several doors |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What’s here, beside foul weather? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | One minded like the weather,<br>Most unquietly. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I know you. Where’s the [[King]]? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Contending with the fretful element;<br>Bids the [[Wind\|wind]] blow the earth into the sea<br>Or swell the curled waters ’bove the main,<br>That things might change or cease; [[Tears\|tears]] his white<br>hair,<br>Which the impetuous blasts, with eyeless rage,<br>Catch in their fury and make nothing of;<br>Strives in his little world of man to outstorm<br>The to-and-fro-conflicting [[Wind\|wind]] and rain.<br>This [[Night\|night]], wherein the cub-drawn bear would couch,<br>The [[Lion\|lion]] and the belly-pinched wolf<br>Keep their fur dry, unbonneted he runs,<br>And bids what will take all. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | But who is with him? |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | None but the [[Fool\|fool]], who labours to outjest<br>His heart-struck injuries. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, I do know you,<br>And dare upon the warrant of my art<br>Commend a dear thing to you. There is division,<br>Although as yet the face of it be covered<br>With mutual cunning, ’twixt Albany and Cornwall;<br>But true it is. From France there comes a power<br>Into this scattered kingdom, who already,<br>Wise in our negligence, have secret feet<br>In some of our best ports, and are at point<br>To show their open banner. Now to you:<br>If on my credit you dare build so far<br>To make your [[Speed\|speed]] to Dover, you shall find<br>Some that will thank you, making just report<br>Of how unnatural and bemadding sorrow<br>The [[King]] hath cause to plain.<br>I am a gentleman of [[Blood\|blood]] and breeding,<br>And from some knowledge and assurance offer<br>This office to you. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | I will talk farther with you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, do not.<br>For confirmation that I am much more<br>Than my out-wall, open this purse, and take<br>What it contains. If you shall see [[Cordelia]]—<br>As [[Fear\|fear]] not but you shall—show her this ring<br>And she will tell you who your fellow is,<br>That yet you do not know. Fie on this [[Storm\|storm]]!<br>I will go seek the [[King]]. |
| FIRST GENTLEMAN | Give me your hand.<br>Have you no more to say? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Few words, but to effect<br>More than all yet: that when we have found the [[King]]—<br>In which endeavour I’ll this way, you that—<br>He that first lights on him holla the other. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sc. 9 |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]]. Enter [[King Lear]] and his [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | Blow, [[Wind\|wind]], and crack your cheeks! Rage, blow,<br>You cataracts and hurricanoes, spout<br>Till you have drenched the steeples, drowned the<br>cocks!<br>You sulphurous and thought-executing fires,<br>Vaunt-couriers to oak-cleaving thunderbolts,<br>Singe my white head; and thou all-shaking thunder,<br>Smite flat the thick rotundity of the world,<br>Crack [[Nature\|nature]]’s mould, all germens spill at once<br>That make ingrateful man. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | O nuncle, [[Court\|court]] holy [[Water\|water]] in a dry [[house]] is better than this rain-[[Water\|water]] out o’ door. Good nuncle, in, and ask thy daughters blessing. Here’s a [[Night\|night]] pities neither wise man nor [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| LEAR | Rumble thy bellyful; spit, [[Fire\|fire]]; spout, rain.<br>Nor rain, [[Wind\|wind]], thunder, [[Fire\|fire]] are my daughters.<br>I tax not you, you elements, with unkindness.<br>I never gave you kingdom, called you children.<br>You owe me no subscription. Why then, let fall<br>Your horrible pleasure. Here I stand your slave,<br>A poor, infirm, weak and despised old man,<br>But yet I call you servile ministers,<br>That have with two pernicious daughters joined<br>Your high engendered battle ‘gainst a head<br>So old and white as this. O, ’tis foul! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | He that has a [[house]] to put his head in has a good headpiece. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | The codpiece that will [[house]] Before the head has any,<br>The head and he shall louse,<br>So beggars marry many.<br><br>The man that makes his toe<br>What he his heart should make<br>Shall have a corn cry woe,<br>And turn his [[Sleep\|sleep]] to wake—<br>for there was never yet fair woman but she made mouths in a glass. |
| LEAR | No, I will be the pattern of all patience. |
| *(didascalia)* | He sits. Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised |
| LEAR | I will say nothing. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Who’s there? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Marry, here’s grace and a codpiece—that’s a wise man and a [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>Alas, sir, sit you here? Things that [[Love\|love]] [[Night\|night]]<br>[[Love]] not such nights as these. The wrathful skies<br>Gallow the very wanderers of the dark<br>And makes them keep their caves. Since I was man<br>Such sheets of [[Fire\|fire]], such bursts of horrid thunder,<br>Such groans of roaring [[Wind\|wind]] and rain I ne’er<br>Remember to have heard. Man’s [[Nature\|nature]] cannot carry<br>The affliction nor the force. |
| LEAR | Let the great gods,<br>That keep this dreadful pother o’er our heads,<br>Find out their enemies now. Tremble, thou wretch<br>That hast within thee undivulgèd crimes<br>Unwhipped of justice; hide thee, thou bloody hand,<br>Thou perjured and thou simular man of virtue<br>That art incestuous; caitiff, in pieces shake,<br>That under covert and convenient seeming<br>Hast practised on man’s life;<br>Close pent-up guilts, rive your concealed centres<br>And cry these dreadful summoners grace.<br>I am a man more sinned against than sinning. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Alack, bare-headed?<br>Gracious my lord, hard by here is a hovel.<br>Some [[Friendship\|friendship]] will it lend you ‘gainst the tempest.<br>Repose you there whilst I to this hard [[house]]-<br>More hard than is the stone whereof ’tis raised,<br>Which even but now, demanding after you,<br>Denied me to come in—return and force<br>Their scanted courtesy. |
| LEAR | My wit begins to turn.<br>(To [[Fool]]) Come on, my boy. How dost, my boy? Art<br>cold?<br>I am cold myself.—Where is this straw, my fellow?<br>The art of our necessities is strange,<br>That can make vile things precious. Come, your<br>hovel.—<br>Poor [[Fool\|fool]] and knave, I have one part of my heart<br>That sorrows yet for thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] sings |
| LEAR | He that has a little tiny wit,<br>With heigh-ho, the [[Wind\|wind]] and the rain,<br>Must make content with his fortunes fit,<br>For the rain it raineth every day. |
| LEAR | True, my good boy. (To [[Kent]]) Come, bring us to this hovel. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 10 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] and [[Edmund]] the bastard, with lights |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, alack, [[Edmund]], I like not this<br>Unnatural dealing. When I desired their leave<br>That I might pity him, they took from me<br>The use of mine own [[house]], charged me on [[Pain\|pain]]<br>Of their displeasure neither to speak of him,<br>Entreat for him, nor any way sustain him. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Most savage and unnatural! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Go to, say you nothing. There’s a division betwixt the Dukes, and a worse matter than that. I have received a letter this [[Night\|night]]—‘tis dangerous to be spoken—I have locked the letter in my closet. These injuries the [[King]] now bears will be revenged [[Home\|home]]. There’s part of a power already landed. We must incline to the [[King]]. I will seek him and privily relieve him. Go you and maintain talk with the Duke, that my [[Charity\|charity]] be not of him perceived. If he ask for me, I am ill and gone to bed. Though I die for’t—as no less is threatened me—the [[King]] my old master must be relieved. There is some strange thing toward. [[Edmund]], pray you be careful. Exit |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | This courtesy, forbid thee, shall the Duke<br>Instantly know, and of that letter too.<br>This seems a fair deserving, and must draw me<br>That which my father loses: no less than all.<br>The younger rises when the old do fall. Exit<br><br>Sc. 11 |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]]. Enter [[King Lear]], the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Here is the place, my lord. Good my lord, enter.<br>The tyranny of the open [[Night\|night]]’s too rough<br>For [[Nature\|nature]] to endure. |
| LEAR | Let me [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my lord, enter here. |
| LEAR | Wilt break my heart? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I had rather break mine own. Good my lord, enter. |
| LEAR | Thou think‘st ’tis much that this contentious [[Storm\|storm]]<br>Invades us to the skin. So ‘tis to thee;<br>But where the greater malady is fixed,<br>The lesser is scarce felt. Thou’dst shun a bear,<br>But if thy flight lay toward the roaring sea<br>Thou‘dst meet the bear i’th’ mouth. When the mind’s<br>free,<br>The [[Body\|body]]’s delicate. This tempest in my mind<br>Doth from my senses take all feeling else<br>Save what beats there: filial ingratitude.<br>Is it not as this mouth should tear this hand<br>For lifting food to’t? But I will punish sure.<br>No, I will weep no more.—<br>In such a [[Night\|night]] as this! O [[Regan]], Gonoril,<br>Your old kind father, whose frank heart gave you all—<br>O, that way [[Madness\|madness]] lies. Let me shun that.<br>No more of that. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my lord, enter. |
| LEAR | Prithee, go in thyself. Seek thy own ease.<br>This tempest will not give me leave to ponder<br>On things would hurt me more; but I’ll go in. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | Poor naked wretches, wheresoe‘er you are,<br>That bide the pelting of this pitiless [[Night\|night]],<br>How shall your houseless heads and unfed sides,<br>Your looped and windowed raggedness, defend you<br>From [[Seasons\|seasons]] such as these? O, I have ta’en<br>Too little care of this. Take physic, pomp,<br>Expose thyself to feel what wretches feel,<br>That thou mayst shake the superflux to them<br>And show the heavens more just. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Come not in here, nuncle; here’s a spirit. Help me, help me! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Give me thy hand. Who’s there? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | A spirit. He says his name’s Poor Tom. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What art thou that dost grumble there in the straw?<br>Come forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Away, the foul fiend follows me. Through the sharp hawthorn blows the cold [[Wind\|wind]]. Go to thy cold bed and warm thee. |
| LEAR | Hast thou given all to thy two daughters,<br>And art thou come to this? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Who gives anything to Poor Tom, whom the foul fiend hath led through [[Fire\|fire]] and through ford and whirlypool, o’er bog and quagmire; that has laid knives under his pillow and halters in his pew, set ratsbane by his potage, made him proud of heart to ride on a bay trotting-[[Horse\|horse]] over four-inched bridges, to course his own [[Shadow\|shadow]] for a traitor. Bless thy five wits, Tom’s a-cold! Bless thee from whirlwinds, star-blasting, and taking. Do Poor Tom some [[Charity\|charity]], whom the foul fiend vexes. There could I have him, now, and there, and there again. |
| LEAR | What, has his daughters brought him to this pass?<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Couldst thou save nothing? Didst thou give<br>them all? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Nay, he reserved a blanket, else we had been all shamed. |
| LEAR | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Now all the plagues that in the pendulous air<br>Hang fated o’er men’s faults fall on thy daughters! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | He hath no daughters, sir. |
| LEAR | [[Death]], traitor! Nothing could have subdued [[Nature\|nature]]<br>To such a lowness but his unkind daughters.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Is it the fashion that discarded fathers<br>Should have thus little [[Mercy\|mercy]] on their flesh?<br>Judicious punishment: ’twas this flesh begot<br>Those pelican daughters. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Pillicock sat on pillicock’s [[Hill\|hill]]; a lo, lo, lo. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | This cold [[Night\|night]] will turn us all to fools and madmen. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Take heed o’th’ foul fiend; obey thy parents; keep thy word justly; swear not; commit not with man’s sworn spouse: set not thy sweet heart on proud array. Tom’s a-cold. |
| LEAR | What hast thou been? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | A servingman, proud in heart and mind, that curled my hair, wore gloves in my cap, served the lust of my mistress’ heart, and did the act of darkness with her; swore as many oaths as I spake words, and broke them in the sweet face of heaven; one that slept in the contriving of lust, and waked to do it. Wine loved I deeply, dice dearly, and in woman out-paramoured the Turk. False of heart, light of ear, bloody of hand; hog in sloth, fox in stealth, wolf in greediness, dog in [[Madness\|madness]], [[Lion\|lion]] in prey. Let not the creaking of shoes nor the rustlings of silks betray thy poor heart to women. Keep thy foot out of brothel, thy hand out of placket, thy pen from lender’s book, and defy the foul fiend. Still through the hawthorn blows the cold [[Wind\|wind]]. Heigh no nonny. Dolphin, my boy, my boy! Cease, let him trot by. |
| LEAR | Why, thou wert better in thy [[Grave\|grave]] than to answer with thy uncovered [[Body\|body]] this extremity of the skies. Is man no more but this? Consider him [[Well\|well]]. Thou owest the worm no silk, the beast no hide, the sheep no wool, the cat no perfume. Here’s three on ’s are sophisticated; thou art the thing itself. Unaccommodated man is no more but such a poor, bare, forked animal as thou art. Off, off, you lendings! Come on, be true. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Prithee, nuncle, be content. This is a naughty [[Night\|night]] to swim in. Now a little [[Fire\|fire]] in a wild field were like an old lecher’s heart—a small spark, all the rest on ’s [[Body\|body]] cold. Look, here comes a walking [[Fire\|fire]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] with a torch |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | This is the foul fiend Flibbertigibbet. He begins at curfew and walks till the first cock. He gives the web and the pin, squinies the eye, and makes the harelip; mildews the white wheat, and hurts the poor creature of earth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Swithin footed thrice the wold,<br>A met the [[Night\|night]] mare and her nine foal;<br>Bid her alight<br>And her troth plight,<br>And aroint thee, [[Witch\|witch]], aroint thee! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>How fares your grace? |
| LEAR | What’s he? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]]) Who’s there? What is’t you seek? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What are you there? Your names? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom, that eats the swimming frog, the toad, the tadpole, the wall-newt and the [[Water\|water]]; that in the fury of his heart, when the foul fiend rages, eats cowdung for salads, swallows the old rat and the ditch-dog, drinks the green mantle of the standing pool; who is whipped from tithing to tithing, and stock-punished, and imprisoned; who hath had three suits to his back, six shirts to his [[Body\|body]],<br>[[Horse]] to ride, and weapon to wear.<br>But mice and rats and such small deer<br>Hath been Tom’s food for seven long year-<br>Beware my follower. Peace, Smolking; peace, thou<br>fiend! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to<br>Lear)<br>What, hath your grace no better company? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The Prince of Darkness is a gentleman;<br>Modo he’s called, and Mahu— |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Lear)<br>Our flesh and [[Blood\|blood]] is grown so vile, my lord,<br>That it doth hate what gets it. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom’s a-cold. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Lear)<br>Go in with me. My duty cannot suffer<br>To obey in all your daughters’ hard commands.<br>Though their injunction be to bar my doors<br>And let this tyrannous [[Night\|night]] take hold upon you,<br>Yet have I ventured to come seek you out<br>And bring you where both food and [[Fire\|fire]] is ready. |
| LEAR | First let me talk with this philosopher.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) What is the cause of thunder? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My good lord,<br>Take his offer; go into the [[house]]. |
| LEAR | I’ll talk a word with this most learnèd Theban.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) What is your study? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | How to prevent the fiend, and to kill vermin. |
| LEAR | Let me ask you one word in private.<br>They converse apart |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>Importune him to go, my lord.<br>His wits begin to unsettle. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Canst thou blame him?<br>His daughters seek his [[Death\|death]]. O, that good [[Kent]],<br>He said it would be thus, poor banished man!<br>Thou sayst the [[King]] grows mad; I’ll tell thee, friend,<br>I am almost mad myself. I had a son,<br>Now outlawed from my [[Blood\|blood]]; a sought my life<br>But lately, very late. I loved him, friend;<br>No father his son dearer. True to tell thee,<br>The grief hath crazed my wits. What a [[Night\|night]]’s this!<br>(To Lear) I do beseech your grace— |
| LEAR | O, cry you [[Mercy\|mercy]].<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Noble philosopher, your company. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Tom’s a-cold. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | In, fellow, there in t’hovel; keep thee warm. |
| LEAR | Come, let’s in all. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | This way, my lord. |
| LEAR | With him!<br>I will keep still with my philosopher. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>Good my lord, soothe him; let him take the fellow. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Take him you on. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent\|KENT]] to [[Edgar]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sirrah, come on. Go along with us. |
| LEAR | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Come, good Athenian. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No words, no words. Hush. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | [[Child]] Roland to the dark tower come, His word was still ‘Fie, fo, and fum; I smell the [[Blood\|blood]] of a British man.’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sc. 12 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall and [[Edmund]] the bastard |
| CORNWALL | I will have my [[Revenge\|revenge]] ere I depart the [[house]]. [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] How, my lord, I may be censured, that [[Nature\|nature]] thus gives way to loyalty, something fears me to think of. |
| CORNWALL | I now perceive it was not altogether your brother’s evil disposition made him seek his [[Death\|death]], but a provoking merit set a-work by a reprovable badness in himself. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | How malicious is my fortune, that I must repent to be just! This is the letter he spoke of, which approves him an intelligent party to the advantages of France. O heavens, that his treason were not, or not I the detector! |
| CORNWALL | Go with me to the Duchess. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | If the matter of this paper be certain, you have mighty business in hand. |
| CORNWALL | True or false, it hath made thee Earl of [[Gloucester]]. Seek out where thy father is, that he may be ready for our apprehension. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] aside If I find him comforting the [[King]], it will stuff his suspicion more fully. (To Cornwall) I will persever in my course of loyalty, though the conflict be sore between that and my [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| CORNWALL | I will lay trust upon thee, and thou shalt find a dearer father in my [[Love\|love]]. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 13 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] and [[King Lear]], the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, Lear’s [[Fool]], and [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Here is better than the open air; take it thankfully. I will piece out the comfort with what addition I can. I will not be long from you. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent\|KENT]] All the power of his wits have given way to impatience; the gods discern your kindness! Exit [[Gloucester]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Frateretto calls me, and tells me Nero is an angler in the lake of darkness. Pray, [[Innocent\|innocent]]; beware the foul fiend. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | (to Lear) Prithee, nuncle, tell me whether a madman be a gentleman or a yeoman. |
| LEAR | A [[King\|king]], a [[King\|king]]! To have a thousand With red burning spits come hissing in upon them! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The foul fiend bites my back. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | (to Lear) He’s mad that trusts in the tameness of a wolf, a [[Horse\|horse]]’s health, a boy’s [[Love\|love]], or a whore’s oath. |
| LEAR | It shall be done. I will arraign them straight. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Edgar]] Come, sit thou here, most learned justicer. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Fool]] Thou sapient sir, sit here.—No, you she- |
| LEAR | foxes— |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Look where he stands and glares. Want’st thou eyes at troll-madam? |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings Come o’er the burn, Bessy, to me. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] sings |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Her boat hath a leak,<br>And she must not speak<br>Why she dares not come over to thee. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The foul fiend haunts Poor Tom in the voice of a [[Nightingale\|nightingale]]. Hoppedance cries in Tom’s belly for two white herring. Croak not, black angel: I have no food for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>How do you, sir? Stand you not so amazed.<br>Will you lie down and rest upon the cushions? |
| LEAR | I’ll see their trial first. Bring in the evidence. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Edgar]] Thou robed man of justice, take thy place; |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Fool]] And thou, his yokefellow of equity, |
| *(didascalia)* | Bench by his side. To [[Kent]] You are o’th’ |
| LEAR | commission,<br>Sit you, too. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Let us deal justly. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sleepest or wakest thou, jolly shepherd?<br>Thy sheep be in the corn,<br>And for one blast of thy minikin mouth<br>Thy sheep shall take no harm.<br>Purr, the cat is grey. |
| LEAR | Arraign her first. ’Tis Gonoril. I here take my oath before this honourable assembly she kicked the poor [[King]] her father. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Come hither, mistress. Is your name Gonoril? |
| LEAR | She cannot deny it. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Cry you [[Mercy\|mercy]], I took you for a join-stool. |
| LEAR | And here’s another, whose warped looks proclaim<br>What store her heart is made on. Stop her there.<br>Arms, arms, [[Sword\|sword]], [[Fire\|fire]], corruption in the place!<br>False justicer, why hast thou let her scape? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bless thy five wits. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>O pity! Sir, where is the patience now<br>That you so oft have boasted to retain? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>My [[Tears\|tears]] begin to take his part so much<br>They’ll mar my counterfeiting. |
| LEAR | The little [[dogs]] and all,<br>Tray, Blanch, and Sweetheart—see, they bark at me. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Tom will throw his head at them.—Avaunt, you curs!<br>Be thy mouth or black or white,<br>Tooth that poisons if it bite,<br>Mastiff, greyhound, mongrel grim,<br>Hound or spaniel, brach or him,<br>Bobtail tyke or trundle-tail,<br>Tom will make them weep and wail;<br>For with throwing thus my head,<br>[[dogs\|Dogs]] leap the hatch, and all are fled.<br>Loudla, doodla! Come, march to wakes and fairs<br>And market towns. Poor Tom, thy horn is dry. |
| LEAR | Then let them anatomize [[Regan]]; see what breeds about her heart. Is there any cause in [[Nature\|nature]] that makes this hardness? (To [[Edgar]]) You, sir, I entertain you for one of my hundred, only I do not like the fashion of your garments. You’ll say they are Persian attire; but let them be changed. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Now, good my lord, lie here a while. |
| LEAR | Make no noise, make no noise. Draw the curtains.<br>So, so, so. We’ll go to supper i’th’ morning. So, so, so.<br>He sleeps. Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[Kent]])<br>Come hither, friend. Where is the [[King]] my master? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Here, sir, but trouble him not; his wits are gone. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Good friend, I prithee take him in thy arms.<br>I have o’erheard a plot of [[Death\|death]] upon him.<br>There is a litter ready. Lay him in’t<br>And drive towards Dover, friend, where thou shalt meet<br>Both welcome and protection. Take up thy master.<br>If thou shouldst dally half an hour, his life,<br>With thine and all that offer to defend him,<br>Stand in assured loss. Take up, take up,<br>And follow me, that will to some provision<br>Give thee quick conduct. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear) Oppressed [[Nature\|nature]] sleeps.<br>This rest might yet have balmed thy broken sinews<br>Which, if convenience will not allow,<br>Stand in hard cure. (To [[Fool]]) Come, help to bear thy<br>master.<br>Thou must not stay behind. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come, come away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | When we our betters see bearing our woes,<br>We scarcely think our miseries our foes.<br>Who [[Alone\|alone]] suffers, suffers most i‘th’ mind,<br>Leaving free things and happy shows behind.<br>But then the mind much sufferance doth o’erskip<br>When grief hath mates, and bearing fellowship.<br>How light and portable my [[Pain\|pain]] seems now,<br>When that which makes me bend, makes the [[King]]<br>bow.<br>He childed as I fathered. Tom, away.<br>Mark the high noises, and thyself bewray<br>When false opinion, whose wrong thoughts defile thee,<br>In thy just proof repeals and reconciles thee.<br>What will hap more tonight, safe scape the [[King]]!<br>Lurk, lurk. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sc. 14 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall and [[Regan]], and Gonoril and [[Edmund]] the bastard, and Servants |
| CORNWALL | (to Gonoril)<br>Post speedily to my lord your husband.<br>Show him this letter. The army of France is landed.<br>(To Servants) Seek out the [[Villain\|villain]] [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Hang him instantly. |
| GONORIL | Pluck out his eyes. |
| CORNWALL | Leave him to my displeasure.—<br>[[Edmund]], keep you our sister company.<br>The revenges we are bound to take upon your traitorous<br>father are not fit for your beholding. Advise the Duke<br>where you are going, to a most festinate preparation;<br>we are bound to the like. Our posts shall be swift, and<br>intelligence betwixt us.—<br>Farewell, dear sister. Farewell, my lord of [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| CORNWALL | How now, where’s the [[King]]? |
| OSWALD | My lord of [[Gloucester]] hath conveyed him hence.<br>Some five- or six-and-thirty of his knights,<br>Hot questants after him, met him at gate,<br>Who, with some other of the lord’s dependants,<br>Are gone with him towards Dover, where they boast<br>To have [[Well\|well]]-armed friends. |
| CORNWALL | Get horses for your mistress. Exit Oswald |
| GONORIL | Farewell, sweet lord, and sister. |
| CORNWALL | [[Edmund]], farewell. Exeunt Gonoril and [[Edmund]]<br>(To Servants) Go seek the traitor [[Gloucester]].<br>Pinion him like a thief; bring him before us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt other Servants |
| CORNWALL | Though we may not pass upon his life<br>Without the form of justice, yet our power<br>Shall do a curtsy to our wrath, which men<br>May blame but not control. Who’s there—the traitor? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] brought in by two or |
| CORNWALL | three |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Ingrateful fox, ’tis he. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants) Bind fast his corky arms. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What means your graces? Good my friends, consider<br>You are my guests. Do me no foul play, friends. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants)<br>Bind him, I say— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Hard, hard! O filthy traitor! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Unmerciful lady as you are, I am true. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants)<br>To this chair bind him. (To [[Gloucester]]) [[Villain]], thou<br>shalt find—<br>[[Regan]] plucks [[Gloucester]]’s beard |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By the kind gods, ’tis most ignobly done,<br>To pluck me by the beard. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | So white, and such a traitor! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Naughty lady,<br>These hairs which thou dost ravish from my chin<br>Will quicken and accuse thee. I am your host.<br>With robbers’ hands my hospitable favours<br>You should not ruffle thus. What will you do? |
| CORNWALL | Come, sir, what letters had you late from France? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Be simple, answerer, for we know the [[Truth\|truth]]. |
| CORNWALL | And what confederacy have you with the traitors<br>Late footed in the kingdom? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | To whose hands<br>You have sent the lunatic [[King]]. Speak. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I have a letter guessingly set down,<br>Which came from one that’s of a neutral heart,<br>And not from one opposed. |
| CORNWALL | Cunning. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And false. |
| CORNWALL | Where hast thou sent the [[King]]? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | To Dover. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Wherefore to Dover? Wast thou not charged at peril— |
| CORNWALL | Wherefore to Dover? Let him first answer that. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I am tied to th’ stake, and I must stand the course. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Wherefore to Dover, sir? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Because I would not see thy cruel nails<br>Pluck out his poor old eyes, nor thy fierce sister<br>In his anointed flesh rash boarish fangs.<br>The sea, with such a [[Storm\|storm]] as his bowed head<br>In hell-black [[Night\|night]] endured, would have buoyed up<br>And quenched the stellèd fires. Yet, poor old heart,<br>He holped the heavens to rage.<br>If wolves had at thy gate howled that dern [[Time\|time]],<br>Thou shouldst have said ‘Good porter, turn the key;<br>All cruels I’ll subscribe.’ But I shall see<br>The winged vengeance overtake such children. |
| CORNWALL | See’t shalt thou never.—Fellows, hold the chair.—<br>Upon those eyes of thine I’ll set my foot. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He that will think to live till he be old<br>Give me some help!—O cruel! O ye gods! |
| *(didascalia)* | Cornwall pulls out one of [[Gloucester]]’s eyes and |
| *(didascalia)* | stamps on it |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to Cornwall)<br>One side will mock another; t’other, too. |
| CORNWALL | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>If you see vengeance— |
| SERVANT | Hold your hand, my lord.<br>I have served you ever since I was a [[Child\|child]],<br>But better service have I never done you<br>Than now to bid you hold. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | How now, you dog! |
| SERVANT | If you did wear a beard upon your chin |
| *(didascalia)* | I’d shake it on this quarrel. To Cornwall What do |
| SERVANT | you mean? |
| CORNWALL | My villein! |
| SERVANT | Why then, come on, and take the chance of anger.<br>They draw and fight |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Regan\|REGAN]] to another Servant |
| SERVANT | Give me thy [[Sword\|sword]]. A peasant stand up thus!<br>She takes a [[Sword\|sword]] and runs at him behind |
| SERVANT | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>O, I am slain, my lord! Yet have you one eye left<br>To see some mischief on him. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Regan]] stabs him again |
| SERVANT | O! He dies |
| CORNWALL | Lest it see more, prevent it. Out, vile jelly! |
| *(didascalia)* | He pulls out [[Gloucester]]’s other eye |
| CORNWALL | Where is thy lustre now? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | All dark and comfortless. Where’s my son [[Edmund]]?<br>[[Edmund]], enkindle all the sparks of [[Nature\|nature]]<br>To quite this horrid act. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Out, [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>Thou call’st on him that hates thee. It was he<br>That made the overture of thy treasons to us,<br>Who is too good to pity thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O, my follies! Then [[Edgar]] was abused.<br>Kind gods, forgive me that, and prosper him! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to Servants)<br>Go thrust him out at gates, and let him smell<br>His way to Dover. (To Cornwall) How is’t, my lord?<br>How look you? |
| CORNWALL | I have received a hurt. Follow me, lady.<br>(To Servants) Turn out that eyeless [[Villain\|villain]]. Throw this<br>slave<br>Upon the dunghill. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more with [[Gloucester]] |
| *(didascalia)* | and the [[Body\|body]] |
| CORNWALL | [[Regan]], I bleed apace.<br>Untimely comes this hurt. Give me your arm. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Cornwall and [[Regan]] |
| SECOND SERVANT | I’ll never care what wickedness I do<br>If this man come to good. |
| THIRD SERVANT | If she live long<br>And in the end meet the old course of [[Death\|death]],<br>Women will all turn monsters. |
| SECOND SERVANT | Let’s follow the old Earl and get the bedlam<br>To lead him where he would. His roguish [[Madness\|madness]]<br>Allows itself to anything. |
| THIRD SERVANT | Go thou. I’ll fetch some flax and whites of eggs<br>To apply to his bleeding face. Now heaven help him! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |
| THIRD SERVANT | Sc. 15 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Yet better thus and known to be contemned<br>Than still contemned and flattered. To be worst,<br>The low’st and most dejected thing of fortune,<br>Stands still in esperance, lives not in [[Fear\|fear]].<br>The lamentable change is from the best;<br>The worst returns to laughter. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] led by an Old Man |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Who’s here? My father, parti-eyed? World, world, O<br>world!<br>But that thy strange mutations make us hate thee,<br>Life would not yield to age. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] stands aside |
| OLD MAN | (to [[Gloucester]]) O my good lord,<br>I have been your tenant and your father’s tenant<br>This fourscore— |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away, get thee away, good friend, be gone.<br>Thy comforts can do me no good at all;<br>Thee they may hurt. |
| OLD MAN | Alack, sir, you cannot see your way. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I have no way, and therefore want no eyes.<br>I stumbled when I saw. Full oft ’tis seen<br>Our means secure us, and our mere defects<br>Prove our commodities. Ah dear son [[Edgar]],<br>The food of thy abused father’s wrath—<br>Might I but live to see thee in my touch<br>I’d say I had eyes again. |
| OLD MAN | How now? Who’s there? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>O gods! Who is’t can say ‘I am at the worst?<br>I am worse than e’er I was. |
| OLD MAN | ’Tis poor mad Tom. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>And worse I may be yet. The worst is not<br>As long as we can say ‘This is the worst.’ |
| OLD MAN | (to [[Edgar]]) Fellow, where goest? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Is it a beggarman? |
| OLD MAN | Madman and beggar too. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A has some reason, else he could not beg.<br>In the last [[Night\|night]]’s [[Storm\|storm]] I such a fellow saw,<br>Which made me think a man a worm. My son<br>Came then into my mind, and yet my mind<br>Was then scarce friends with him. I have heard more<br>since.<br>As flies to wanton boys are we to th’ gods;<br>They kill us for their sport. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside) How should this be?<br>Bad is the trade that must play [[Fool\|fool]] to sorrow,<br>Ang’ring itself and others. |
| *(didascalia)* | He comes forward |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bless thee, master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Is that the naked fellow? |
| OLD MAN | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Then prithee, get thee gone. If for my sake<br>Thou wilt o‘ertake us hence a mile or twain<br>I’th’ way toward Dover, do it for ancient [[Love\|love]],<br>And bring some covering for this naked [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Who I’ll entreat to lead me. |
| OLD MAN | Alack, sir, he is mad. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | ’Tis the [[Time\|time]]’s plague when madmen lead the blind.<br>Do as I bid thee; or rather do thy pleasure.<br>Above the rest, be gone. |
| OLD MAN | I’ll bring him the best ’parel that I have,<br>Come on’t what will. Exit |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sirrah, naked fellow! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom’s a-cold. I cannot [[Dance\|dance]] it farther. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come hither, fellow. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bless thy sweet eyes, they bleed. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Know’st thou the way to Dover? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Both stile and gate, horseway and footpath. Poor Tom hath been scared out of his good wits. Bless thee, goodman, from the foul fiend. Five fiends have been in Poor Tom at once, as Obidicut of lust, Hobbididence prince of dumbness, Mahu of stealing, Modo of murder, Flibbertigibbet of mocking and mowing, who since possesses chambermaids and waiting-women. So bless thee, master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Here, take this purse, thou whom the heavens’ plagues<br>Have humbled to all strokes. That I am wretched<br>Makes thee the happier. Heavens deal so still.<br>Let the superfluous and lust-dieted man<br>That stands your ordinance, that will not see<br>Because he does not feel, feel your power quickly.<br>So distribution should undo excess,<br>And each man have enough. Dost thou know Dover? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Ay, master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | There is a cliff whose high and bending head<br>Looks saucily in the confined deep.<br>Bring me but to the very brim of it<br>And I’ll repair the misery thou dost bear<br>With something rich about me. From that place<br>I shall no leading need. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me thy arm.<br>Poor Tom shall lead thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] guiding [[Gloucester]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sc. 16 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at one door Gonoril and [[Edmund]] the bastard |
| GONORIL | Welcome, my lord. I marvel our mild husband<br>Not met us on the way. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter at another door Oswald the steward |
| GONORIL | Now, where’s your master? |
| OSWALD | Madam, within; but never man so changed.<br>I told him of the army that was landed;<br>He smiled at it. I told him you were coming;<br>His answer was ‘The worse.’ Of [[Gloucester]]’s treachery<br>And of the loyal service of his son<br>When I informed him, then he called me sot,<br>And told me I had turned the wrong side out.<br>What he should most defy seems pleasant to him;<br>What like, offensive. |
| GONORIL | (to [[Edmund]]) Then shall you go no further.<br>It is the cowish terror of his spirit<br>That dares not undertake. He’ll not feel wrongs<br>Which tie him to an answer. Our wishes on the way<br>May prove effects. Back, [[Edmund]], to my brother.<br>Hasten his musters and conduct his powers.<br>I must change arms at [[Home\|home]], and give the distaff<br>Into my husband’s hands. This trusty servant<br>Shall pass between us. Ere long you are like to hear,<br>If you dare venture in your own behalf,<br>A mistress’s command. Wear this. Spare speech.<br>Decline your head. This kiss, if it durst speak,<br>Would stretch thy spirits up into the air. |
| *(didascalia)* | she kisses him |
| GONORIL | Conceive, and fare you [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Edmund\|EDMUND]] | Yours in the ranks of [[Death\|death]]. |
| GONORIL | My most dear [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edmund]] |
| GONORIL | To thee a woman’s services are due;<br>My foot usurps my [[Body\|body]]. |
| OSWALD | Madam, here comes my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Albany |
| GONORIL | I have been worth the whistling. |
| ALBANY | O Gonoril,<br>You are not worth the [[Dust\|dust]] which the rude [[Wind\|wind]]<br>Blows in your face. I [[Fear\|fear]] your disposition.<br>That [[Nature\|nature]] which contemns it origin<br>Cannot be bordered certain in itself.<br>She that herself will sliver and disbranch<br>From her material sap perforce must wither,<br>And come to deadly use. |
| GONORIL | No more. The text is foolish. |
| ALBANY | Wisdom and goodness to the vile seem vile;<br>Filths savour but themselves. What have you done?<br>Tigers, not daughters, what have you performed?<br>A father, and a gracious, aged man,<br>Whose reverence even the head-lugged bear would<br>lick,<br>Most barbarous, most degenerate, have you madded.<br>Could my good-brother suffer you to do it—<br>A man, a prince by him so benefacted?<br>If that the heavens do not their visible spirits<br>Send quickly down to tame these vile offences,<br>It will come,<br>Humanity must perforce prey on itself,<br>Like monsters of the deep. |
| GONORIL | Milk-livered man,<br>That bear‘st a cheek for blows, a head for wrongs;<br>Who hast not in thy brows an eye discerning<br>Thine [[Honour\|honour]] from thy [[Suffering\|suffering]]; that not know’st<br>Fools do those villains pity who are punished<br>Ere they have done their mischief: where’s thy drum?<br>France spreads his banners in our noiseless land,<br>With plumed helm thy flaxen biggin threats,<br>Whiles thou, a moral [[Fool\|fool]], sits still and cries<br>‘Alack, why does he so?’ |
| ALBANY | See thyself, devil.<br>Proper deformity shows not in the fiend<br>So horrid as in woman. |
| GONORIL | O vain [[Fool\|fool]]! 60 |
| ALBANY | Thou changed and [[Self\|self]]-covered thing, for shame<br>Bemonster not thy feature. Were’t my fitness<br>To let these hands obey my [[Blood\|blood]],<br>They are apt enough to dislocate and tear<br>Thy flesh and bones. Howe’er thou art a fiend,<br>A woman’s shape doth shield thee. |
| GONORIL | Marry your manhood, mew— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Second Gentleman |
| ALBANY | What news? |
| *(didascalia)* | SECOND GENTLEMAN |
| ALBANY | O my good lord, the Duke of Cornwall’s dead,<br>Slain by his servant going to put out<br>The other eye of [[Gloucester]]. |
| ALBANY | [[Gloucester]]’s eyes? |
| *(didascalia)* | FSECOND GENTLEMAN |
| ALBANY | A servant that he bred, thralled with remorse,<br>Opposed against the act, bending his [[Sword\|sword]]<br>To his great master, who thereat enraged<br>Flew on him, and amongst them felled him dead,<br>But not without that harmful stroke which since<br>Hath plucked him after. |
| ALBANY | This shows you are above,<br>You justicers, that these our nether crimes<br>So speedily can venge. But O, poor [[Gloucester]]!<br>Lost he his other eye? |
| *(didascalia)* | SECOND GENTLEMAN Both, both, my lord. 80 |
| ALBANY | (To Gonoril) This letter, madam, craves a speedy<br>answer.<br>’Tis from your sister. |
| GONORIL | (aside) One way I like this [[Well\|well]];<br>But being widow, and my [[Gloucester]] with her,<br>May all the building on my fancy pluck<br>Upon my hateful life. Another way<br>The news is not so took.—I’ll read and answer. Exit |
| ALBANY | Where was his son when they did take his eyes? |
| *(didascalia)* | ECONDGENTLEMAN |
| ALBANY | Come with my lady hither. |
| ALBANY | He is not here. |
| *(didascalia)* | ECONDGENTLEMAN |
| ALBANY | No, my good lord; I met him back again. |
| ALBANY | Knows he the wickedness? 90 |
| *(didascalia)* | ECONDGENTLEMAN |
| ALBANY | Ay, my good lord; ’twas he informed against him,<br>And quit the [[house]] on purpose that their punishment<br>Might have the freer course. |
| ALBANY | [[Gloucester]], I live<br>To thank thee for the [[Love\|love]] thou showed’st the [[King]],<br>And to [[Revenge\|revenge]] thy eyes.—Come hither, friend<br>Tell me what more thou knowest. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 17 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and First Gentleman |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why the [[King of France]] is so suddenly gone back know you no reason? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Something he left imperfect in the state<br>Which, since his coming forth, is thought of; which<br>Imports to the kingdom so much [[Fear\|fear]] and danger<br>That his personal return was most required<br>And necessary. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Who hath he left behind him general? |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST NTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | The Maréchal of France, Monsieur la Far. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Did your letters pierce the [[Queen]] to any demonstration of grief? II |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ay, sir. She took them, read them in my presence,<br>And now and then an ample tear trilled down<br>Her delicate cheek. It seemed she was a [[Queen\|queen]]<br>Over her passion who, most [[Rebel\|rebel]]-like,<br>Sought to be [[King\|king]] o’er her. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | O, then it moved her. |
| *(didascalia)* | IRSTGENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Not to a rage. Patience and sorrow strove<br>Who should express her goodliest. You have seen<br>Sunshine and rain at once; her smiles and [[Tears\|tears]]<br>Were like, a better way. Those happy smilets<br>That played on her ripe lip seemed not to know<br>What guests were in her eyes, which parted thence<br>As pearls from diamonds dropped. In brief,<br>Sorrow would be a rarity most beloved<br>If all could so become it. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Made she no verbal question? |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | [[Faith]], once or twice she heaved the name of ‘father’<br>Pantingly forth as if it pressed her heart,<br>Cried ‘Sisters, sisters, shame of ladies, sisters,<br>[[Kent]], father, sisters, what, i‘th’ [[Storm\|storm]], i’th’ [[Night\|night]],<br>Let piety not be believed!’ There she shook<br>The holy [[Water\|water]] from her heavenly eyes<br>And clamour mastered, then away she started<br>To deal with grief [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | It is the [[Stars\|stars]],<br>The [[Stars\|stars]] above us govern our conditions,<br>Else one [[Self\|self]] mate and make could not beget<br>Such different issues. You spoke not with her since? |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN No. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Was this before the [[King]] returned? |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN No, since. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | [[Well]], sir, the poor distressed Lear’s i’th’ town,<br>Who sometime in his better tune remembers<br>What we are come about, and by no means<br>Will yield to see his daughter. |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST NTLEMAN Why, good sir? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A sovereign shame so elbows him: his own unkindness,<br>That stripped her from his benediction, turned her<br>To foreign casualties, gave her dear rights<br>To his dog-hearted daughters—these things sting<br>His mind so venomously that burning shame<br>Detains him from [[Cordelia]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN Alack, poor gentleman! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Of Albany’s and Cornwall’s powers you heard not? |
| *(didascalia)* | IRST ENTLEMAN ’Tis so; they are afoot. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | [[Well]], sir, I’ll bring you to our master Lear,<br>And leave you to attend him. Some dear cause<br>Will in concealment wrap me up a while.<br>When I am known aright you shall not grieve<br>Lending me this acquaintance. I pray you go<br>Along with me. Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 18 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]], a Doctor, and others |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Alack, ’tis he! Why, he was met even now,<br>As mad as the racked sea, singing aloud,<br>Crowned with rank fumitor and furrow-weeds,<br>With burdocks, hemlock, nettles, cuckoo-[[Flowers\|flowers]],<br>Darnel, and all the idle weeds that grow<br>In our sustaining corn. The centuries send forth.<br>Search every acre in the high-grown field,<br>And bring him to our eye. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | What can man’s wisdom<br>In the restoring his bereaved sense,<br>He that can help him<br>Take all my outward worth. |
| DOCTOR | There is means, madam.<br>Our foster-[[Nurse\|nurse]] of [[Nature\|nature]] is repose,<br>The which he lacks. That to provoke in him<br>Are many simples operative, whose power<br>Will close the eye of anguish. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | All blest [[Secrets\|secrets]],<br>All you unpublished virtues of the earth,<br>[[Spring]] with my [[Tears\|tears]], be aidant and remediate<br>In the good man’s distress!—Seek, seek for him,<br>Lest his ungoverned rage dissolve the life<br>That wants the means to lead it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | News, madam.<br>The British powers are marching hitherward. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | ’Tis known before; our preparation stands<br>In expectation of them.—O dear father,<br>It is thy business that I go about;<br>Therefore great France<br>My mourning and important [[Tears\|tears]] hath pitied.<br>No blown [[Ambition\|ambition]] doth our arms incite,<br>But [[Love\|love]], dear [[Love\|love]], and our aged father’s right.<br>Soon may I hear and see him! Exeunt<br><br>Sc. 19 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Regan]] and Oswald, Gonoril’s steward |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | But are my brother’s powers set forth? |
| OSWALD | Ay, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Himself in person? |
| OSWALD | Madam, with much ado.<br>Your sister is the better soldier. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Lord [[Edmund]] spake not with your lord at [[Home\|home]]? |
| OSWALD | No, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What might import my sister’s letters to him? |
| OSWALD | I know not, lady. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | [[Faith]], he is posted hence on serious matter.<br>It was great ignorance, [[Gloucester]]’s eyes being out,<br>To let him live. Where he arrives he moves<br>All hearts against us. [[Edmund]], I think, is gone,<br>In pity of his misery, to dispatch<br>His ‘nighted life, moreover to descry<br>The strength o’th’ army. |
| OSWALD | I must needs after with my letters, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Our troop sets forth tomorrow. Stay with us.<br>The ways are dangerous. |
| OSWALD | I may not, madam.<br>My lady charged my duty in this business. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why should she write to [[Edmund]]? Might not you<br>Transport her purposes by word? Belike—<br>Something, I know not what. I’ll [[Love\|love]] thee much:<br>Let me unseal the letter. |
| OSWALD | Madam, I’d rather— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I know your lady does not [[Love\|love]] her husband.<br>I am sure of that, and at her late being here<br>She gave strange oeillades and most speaking looks<br>To noble [[Edmund]]. I know you are of her bosom. |
| OSWALD | I, madam? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I speak in understanding, for I know’t.<br>Therefore I do advise you take this note.<br>My lord is dead. [[Edmund]] and I have talked,<br>And more convenient is he for my hand<br>Than for your lady’s. You may gather more.<br>If you do find him, pray you give him this,<br>And when your mistress hears thus much from you,<br>I pray [[Desire\|desire]] her call her wisdom to her.<br>So, farewell.<br>If you do chance to hear of that blind traitor,<br>Preferment falls on him that cuts him off. |
| OSWALD | Would I could meet him, madam. I would show<br>What lady I do follow. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Fare thee [[Well\|well]]. Exeunt severally<br><br>Sc. 20 |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] disguised as a peasant, with a staff, guiding the blind [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | When shall we come to th’ top of that same [[Hill\|hill]]? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | You do climb up it now. Look how we labour. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Methinks the ground is even. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Horrible steep.<br>Hark, do you hear the sea? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No, truly. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Why, then your other senses grow imperfect<br>By your eyes’ anguish. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | So may it be indeed.<br>Methinks thy voice is altered, and thou speak’st<br>With better phrase and matter than thou didst. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | You’re much deceived. In nothing am I changed<br>But in my garments. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Methinks you’re better spoken. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Come on, sir, here’s the place. Stand still. How fearful<br>And dizzy ’tis to cast one’s eyes so low!<br>The crows and choughs that wing the midway air<br>Show scarce so gross as beetles. Halfway down<br>Hangs one that gathers samphire, dreadful trade!<br>Methinks he seems no bigger than his head.<br>The fishermen that walk upon the beach<br>Appear like mice, and yon tall anchoring barque<br>Diminished to her cock, her cock a buoy<br>Almost too small for sight. The murmuring surge<br>That on the unnumbered idle pebble chafes<br>Cannot be heard, it’s so high. I’ll look no more,<br>Lest my brain turn and the deficient sight<br>Topple down headlong. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Set me where you stand. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your hand. You are now within a foot<br>Of th’extreme verge. For all beneath the [[Moon\|moon]]<br>Would I not leap upright. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let go my hand.<br>Here, friend, ’s another purse; in it a [[Jewel\|jewel]]<br>[[Well]] worth a poor man’s taking. Fairies and gods<br>Prosper it with thee! Go thou farther off.<br>Bid me farewell, and let me hear thee going. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Now fare you [[Well\|well]], good sir.<br>He stands aside |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | With all my heart. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>Why I do trifle thus with his [[Despair\|despair]]<br>Is done to cure it. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O you mighty gods,<br>He kneels<br>This world I do renounce, and in your sights<br>Shake patiently my great affliction off!<br>If I could bear it longer, and not fall<br>To quarrel with your great opposeless wills,<br>My snuff and loathed part of [[Nature\|nature]] should<br>Burn itself out. If [[Edgar]] live, O bless him!—<br>Now, fellow, fare thee [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Gone, sir. Farewell.<br>[[Gloucester]] falls forward<br>(Aside) And yet I know not how conceit may rob<br>The treasury of life, when life itself<br>Yields to the theft. Had he been where he thought,<br>By this had thought been past.—Ative or dead?<br>(To [[Gloucester]]) Ho you, sir; hear you, sir? Speak.<br>(Aside) Thus might he pass indeed. Yet he revives.<br>(To [[Gloucester]]) What are you, sir? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away, and let me die. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Hadst thou been aught but goss‘mer, feathers, air,<br>So many fathom down precipitating<br>Thou hadst shivered like an egg. But thou dost breathe,<br>Hast heavy substance, bleed’st not, speak’st, art sound.<br>Ten masts a-length make not the altitude<br>Which thou hast perpendicularly fell.<br>Thy life’s a miracle. Speak yet again. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But have I fallen, or no? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | From the dread summit of this chalky bourn.<br>Look up a-height. The shrill-gorged lark so far<br>Cannot be seen or heard. Do but look up. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, I have no eyes.<br>Is wretchedness deprived that benefit<br>To end itself by [[Death\|death]]? ’Twas yet some comfort<br>When misery could beguile the [[Tyrant\|tyrant]]’s rage<br>And frustrate his proud will. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your arm.<br>Up. So, how now? Feel you your legs? You stand. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Too [[Well\|well]], too [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | This is above all strangeness.<br>Upon the [[Crown\|crown]] of the cliff what thing was that<br>Which parted from you? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A poor unfortunate beggar. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | As I stood here below, methoughts his eyes<br>Were two full moons. A had a thousand noses,<br>Horns whelked and waved like the enridged sea.<br>It was some fiend. Therefore, thou happy father,<br>Think that the clearest gods, who made their honours<br>Of men’s impossibilities, have preserved thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I do remember now. Henceforth I’ll bear<br>Affliction till it do cry out itself<br>‘Enough, enough,’ and die. That thing you speak of,<br>I took it for a man. Often would it say<br>‘The fiend, the fiend!’ He led me to that place. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bear free and patient thoughts. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] mad, crowned with weeds and |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Flowers\|flowers]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | But who comes here?<br>The safer sense will ne’er accommodate<br>His master thus. |
| LEAR | No, they cannot touch me for coining. I am the [[King]] himself. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | O thou side-piercing sight! |
| LEAR | [[Nature]] is above art in that respect. There’s your press-money. That fellow handles his bow like a crow-keeper. Draw me a clothier’s yard. Look, look, a mouse! Peace, peace, this toasted [[cheese]] will do it. There’s my gauntlet. I’ll prove it on a giant. Bring up the brown bills. O, [[Well\|well]] flown, bird, in the air. Hal Give the word. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sweet marjoram. |
| LEAR | Pass. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I know that voice. |
| LEAR | Ha, Gonorill Ha, [[Regan]]! They flattered me like a dog, and told me I had white hairs in my beard ere the black ones were there. To say ‘ay’ and ‘no’ to everything I said ‘ay’ and ‘no’ to was no good divinity. When the rain came to wet me once, and the [[Wind\|wind]] to make me chatter, when the thunder would not peace at my bidding, there I found them, there I smelt them out. Go to, they are not men of their words. They told me I was everything; ’tis a lie, I am not ague-proof. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The trick of that voice I do [[Well\|well]] remember.<br>Is’t not the [[King]]? |
| LEAR | Ay, every inch a [[King\|king]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester]] kneels |
| LEAR | When I do stare, see how the subject quakes!<br>I pardon that man’s life. What was thy cause?<br>Adultery? Thou shalt not die for adultery.<br>No, the wren goes to‘t, and the small gilded fly<br>Does lecher in my sight.<br>Let copulation thrive, for [[Gloucester]]’s bastard son<br>Was kinder to his father than my daughters<br>Got ’tween the lawful sheets. To‘t, luxury, pell-mell,<br>For I lack soldiers. Behold yon simp’ring dame,<br>Whose face between her forks presageth [[Snow\|snow]],<br>That minces virtue, and does shake the head<br>To hear of pleasure’s name:<br>The fitchew nor the soiled [[Horse\|horse]] goes to’t<br>With a more riotous appetite. Down from the waist<br>They’re centaurs, though women all above.<br>But to the girdle do the gods inherit;<br>Beneath is all the fiend’s. There’s hell, there’s<br>darkness,<br>There’s the sulphury pit, burning, scalding,<br>Stench, consummation. Fie, fie, fie; pah, pah!<br>Give me an ounce of civet, good apothecary,<br>To sweeten my [[Imagination\|imagination]].<br>There’s money for thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O, let me kiss that hand! |
| LEAR | Here, wipe it first; it smells of [[Mortality\|mortality]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O ruined piece of [[Nature\|nature]]! This great world<br>Shall so wear out to naught. Do you know me? |
| LEAR | I remember thy eyes [[Well\|well]] enough. Dost thou squiny on me?<br>No, do thy worst, blind Cupid, I’ll not [[Love\|love]].<br>Read thou that challenge. Mark the penning of’t. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Were all the letters suns, I could not see one. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>I would not take this from report; it is,<br>And my heart breaks at it. |
| LEAR | (to [[Gloucester]]) Read. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What—with the case of eyes? |
| LEAR | O ho, are you there with me? No eyes in your head, nor no money in your purse? Your eyes are in a heavy case, your purse in a light; yet you see how this world goes. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I see it feelingly. |
| LEAR | What, art mad? A man may see how the world goes with no eyes; look with thy ears. See how yon justice rails upon yon simple thief. Hark in thy ear: handy-dandy, which is the thief, which is the justice? Thou hast seen a farmer’s dog bark at a beggar? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, sir. |
| LEAR | An the creature run from the cur, there thou mightst behold the great image of authority. A dog’s obeyed in office.<br>Thou rascal beadle, hold thy bloody hand.<br>Why dost thou lash that whore? Strip thine own back.<br>Thy [[Blood\|blood]] as hotly lusts to use her in that kind<br>For which thou whip’st her. The usurer hangs the<br>cozener.<br>Through tattered rags small vices do appear;<br>Robes and furred gowns hides all. Get thee glass eyes,<br>And, like a scurvy politician, seem<br>To see the things thou dost not. No [[Tears\|tears]], now.<br>Pull off my boots. Harder, harder! So. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>O, matter and impertinency mixed—<br>Reason in [[Madness\|madness]]! |
| LEAR | If thou wilt weep my fortune, take my eyes.<br>I know thee [[Well\|well]] enough: thy name is [[Gloucester]].<br>Thou must be patient. We came crying hither.<br>Thou know’st the first [[Time\|time]] that we smell the air<br>We wail and cry. I will preach to thee. Mark me. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, alack, the day! |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR removing his [[Crown\|crown]] of weeds |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | When we are born, we cry that we are come<br>To this great stage of fools. This’ a good block.<br>It were a delicate stratagem to shoe<br>A troop of [[Horse\|horse]] with felt; and when I have stole<br>upon<br>These son-in-laws, then kill, kill, kill, kill, kill, kill! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter three Gentlemen |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O, here he is. Lay hands upon him, sirs.<br>(To Lear) Your most dear— |
| LEAR | No rescue? What, a prisoner? I am e’en<br>The natural [[Fool\|fool]] of fortune. Use me [[Well\|well]].<br>You shall have ransom. Let me have a surgeon;<br>I am cut to the brains. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN You shall have anything. |
| LEAR | No seconds? All myself?<br>Why, this would make a man a man of salt,<br>To use his eyes for [[Garden\|garden]] [[Water\|water]]-pots,<br>Ay, and laying [[Autumn\|autumn]]’s [[Dust\|dust]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN Good sir— |
| LEAR | I will die bravely, like a bridegroom.<br>What, I will be jovial. Come, come,<br>I am a [[King\|king]], my masters, know you that? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | You are a royal one, and we obey you. |
| LEAR | Then there’s life in’t. Nay, an you get it, you shall get it with running. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit running, pursued by two Gentlemen |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | A sight most pitiful in the meanest wretch,<br>Past speaking in a [[King\|king]]. Thou hast one daughter<br>Who redeems [[Nature\|nature]] from the general curse<br>Which twain hath brought her to. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Hail, gentle sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN Sir, [[Speed\|speed]] you. What’s your will? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Do you hear aught of a battle toward? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Most sure and vulgar, everyone hears that<br>That can distinguish sense. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | But, by your favour,<br>How near’s the other army? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Near and on speedy foot, the main; descriers<br>Stands on the hourly thoughts. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I thank you, sir. That’s all. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Though that the [[Queen]] on special cause is here,<br>Her army is moved on. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I thank you, sir. Exit Gentleman |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | You ever gentle gods, take my breath from me.<br>Let not my worser spirit tempt me again<br>To die before you please. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | [[Well]] pray you, father. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now, good sir, what are you? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | A most poor man, made lame by fortune’s blows,<br>Who by the art of known and feeling sorrows<br>Am pregnant to good pity. Give me your hand,<br>I’ll lead you to some biding. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] rising Hearty thanks. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The bounty and the benison of heaven<br>To send thee boot to boot. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| OSWALD | A proclaimed prize! Most happy!<br>That eyeless head of thine was first framed flesh<br>To raise my fortunes. Thou most unhappy traitor,<br>Briefly thyself remember. The [[Sword\|sword]] is out<br>That must destroy thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now let thy friendly hand<br>Put strength enough to’t. |
| OSWALD | (to [[Edgar]]) Wherefore, bold peasant,<br>Durst thou support a published traitor? Hence,<br>Lest the infection of his fortune take<br>Like hold on thee. Let go his arm. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | ‘Chill not let go, sir, without ’cagion. |
| OSWALD | Let go, slave, or thou diest. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Good gentleman, go your gate. Let poor volk pass. An ‘chud have been swaggered out of my life, it would not have been so long by a vortnight. Nay, come not near the old man. Keep out, ’che vor’ ye, or I’ll try whether your [[Costard\|costard]] or my baton be the harder; I’ll be plain with you. |
| OSWALD | Out, dunghill!<br>They fight |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | ’Chill pick your teeth, sir. Come, no matter for your foins. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] knocks him down |
| OSWALD | Slave, thou hast slain me. [[Villain]], take my purse.<br>If ever thou wilt thrive, bury my [[Body\|body]],<br>And give the letters which thou find’st about me<br>To [[Edmund]], Earl of [[Gloucester]]. Seek him out<br>Upon the British party. O untimely [[Death\|death]]! [[Death]]!<br>He dies |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I know thee [[Well\|well]]—a serviceable [[Villain\|villain]],<br>As duteous to the vices of thy mistress<br>As badness would [[Desire\|desire]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What, is he dead? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sit you down, father. Rest you.<br>[[Gloucester]] sits<br>Let’s see his pockets. These letters that he speaks of<br>May be my friends. He’s dead; I am only sorrow<br>He had no other deathsman. Let us see.<br>Leave, gentle wax; and manners, blame us not.<br>To know our enemies’ minds we’d rip their hearts;<br>Their papers is more lawful.<br>He reads a letter<br>‘Let your reciprocal vows be remembered. You have<br>many opportunities to cut him off. If your will want<br>not, [[Time\|time]] and place will be fruitfully offered. There is<br>nothing done if he return the conqueror; then am I<br>the prisoner, and his bed my jail, from the loathed<br>warmth whereof, deliver me, and supply the place for<br>your labour.<br>Your—wife, so I would say—your affectionate<br>servant, and for you her own for venture,<br>Gonoril.’<br>O indistinguished space of woman’s wit—<br>A plot upon her virtuous husband’s life,<br>And the exchange my brother!—Here in the sands<br>Thee I’ll rake up, the post unsanctified<br>Of murderous lechers, and in the mature [[Time\|time]]<br>With this ungracious paper strike the sight<br>Of the [[Death\|death]]-practised Duke. For him ’tis [[Well\|well]]<br>That of thy [[Death\|death]] and business I can tell.<br>[Exit with the [[Body\|body]]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is mad. How stiff is my vile sense,<br>That I stand up and have ingenious feeling<br>Of my huge sorrows! Better I were distraught;<br>So should my thoughts be fenced from my griefs,<br>And woes by wrong imaginations lose<br>The knowledge of themselves.<br>A drum afar off. [Enter [[Edgar]]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your hand.<br>Far off methinks I hear the beaten drum.<br>Come, father, I’ll bestow you with a friend. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] guiding [[Gloucester]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Sc. 21 Soft music. Enter [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]], and the Earl of [[Kent]], disguised |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O thou good [[Kent]],<br>How shall I live and work to match thy goodness?<br>My life will be too short, and every measure fail me. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | To be acknowledged, madam, is o’erpaid.<br>All my reports go with the modest [[Truth\|truth]],<br>Nor more, nor clipped, but so. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Be better suited.<br>These weeds are memories of those worser hours.<br>I prithee put them off. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Pardon me, dear madam.<br>Yet to be known shortens my made intent.<br>My boon I make it that you know me not<br>Till [[Time\|time]] and I think meet. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Then be’t so, my good lord.<br>[Enter the Doctor and First Gentleman]<br>How does the [[King]]? |
| DOCTOR | Madam, sleeps still. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O you kind gods,<br>Cure this great breach in his abused [[Nature\|nature]];<br>The untuned and hurrying senses O [[Wind\|wind]] up<br>Of this [[Child\|child]]-changed father! |
| DOCTOR | So please your majesty<br>That we may wake the [[King]]? He hath slept long. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Be governed by your knowledge, and proceed<br>I’th’ sway of your own will. Is he arrayed?<br>[FIRST GENTLEMAN]<br>Ay, madam. In the heaviness of his [[Sleep\|sleep]]<br>We put fresh garments on him.<br>[DOCTOR]<br>Good madam, be by when we do awake him.<br>I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not of his temperance. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Very [[Well\|well]]. |
| DOCTOR | Please you draw near. Louder the music there!<br>[[King Lear]] is [discovered] asleep |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O my dear father, restoration hang<br>Thy medicine on my lips, and let this kiss<br>Repair those violent harms that my two sisters<br>Have in thy reverence made! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Kind and dear princess! |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Had you not been their father, these white flakes<br>Had challenged pity of them. Was this a face<br>To be exposed against the warring winds,<br>To stand against the deep dread-bolted thunder<br>In the most terrible and nimble stroke<br>Of quick [[Cross\|cross]]-[[Lightning\|lightning]], to watch—poor perdu—<br><br>With this thin helm? Mine injurer’s mean‘st dog,<br>Though he had bit me, should have stood that [[Night\|night]]<br>Against my [[Fire\|fire]]. And wast thou fain, poor father,<br>To hovel thee with swine and rogues forlorn<br>In short and musty straw? Alack, alack,<br>’Tis [[Wonder\|wonder]] that thy life and wits at once<br>Had not concluded all! (To the Doctor) He wakes.<br>Speak to him. |
| DOCTOR | Madam, do you; ’tis fittest. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear)<br>How does my royal lord? How fares your majesty? |
| LEAR | You do me wrong to take me out o’th’ [[Grave\|grave]].<br>Thou art a [[Soul\|soul]] in bliss, but I am bound<br>Upon a [[Wheel\|wheel]] of [[Fire\|fire]], that mine own [[Tears\|tears]]<br>Do scald like molten lead. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Sir, know me. |
| LEAR | You’re a spirit, I know. Where did you die? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to the Doctor) Still, still far wide! |
| DOCTOR | He’s scarce awake. Let him [[Alone\|alone]] a while. |
| LEAR | Where have I been? Where am I? Fair daylight?<br>I am mightily abused. I should e’en die with pity<br>To see another thus. I know not what to say.<br>I will not swear these are my hands. Let’s see:<br>I feel this pin prick. Would I were assured<br>Of my condition. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (kneeling) O look upon me, sir,<br>And hold your hands in benediction o’er me.<br>No, sir, you must not kneel. |
| LEAR | Pray do not mock.<br>I am a very foolish, fond old man,<br>Fourscore and upward, and to deal plainly,<br>I [[Fear\|fear]] I am not in my perfect mind.<br>Methinks I should know you, and know this man;<br>Yet I am doubtful, for I am mainly ignorant<br>What place this is; and all the skill I have<br>Remembers not these garments; nor I know not<br>Where I did lodge last [[Night\|night]]. Do not laugh at me,<br>For as I am a man, I think this lady<br>To be my [[Child\|child]], [[Cordelia]]. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | And so I am. |
| LEAR | Be your [[Tears\|tears]] wet? Yes, [[Faith\|faith]]. I pray, weep not.<br>If you have [[Poison\|poison]] for me, I will drink it.<br>I know you do not [[Love\|love]] me; for your sisters<br>Have, as I do remember, done me wrong.<br>You have some cause; they have not. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | No cause, no cause. |
| LEAR | Am I in France? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | In your own kingdom, sir. |
| LEAR | Do not abuse me. |
| DOCTOR | Be comforted, good madam. The great rage<br>You see is cured in him, and yet it is danger<br>To make him even o’er the [[Time\|time]] he has lost.<br>[[Desire]] him to go in; trouble him no more<br>Till further settling. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear) Will’t please your highness walk? |
| LEAR | You must bear with me.<br>Pray now, forget and forgive. I am old<br>And foolish. Exeunt all but [[Kent]] and [First] Gentleman<br>[FIRST] GENTLEMAN Holds it true, sir, that the Duke Of Cornwall was so slain? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Most certain, sir.<br>[FIRST] GENTLEMAN<br>Who is conductor of his people? |
